) since
\alpha^{F}
and
\underline{\alpha}^{F}
only verifies
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
and
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}
equations. This complicates the situation substantially since we need a separate energy norm for these terms and suitably constructed Bianchi-pair integration is utilized in a hierarchical manner to complete the argument. Once we have obtained the final estimates (that are independent of the initial bootstrap constant chosen and only depend on the initial data), we may choose the initial bootstrap constant accordingly to close the argument. A novelty in our proof of the estimates is the use of fully gauge-invariant norms of the Yang-Mills curvature components instead of working in a particular choice of gauge.

Remark 2

We note that the gauge group for the Yang-Mills theory is compact allowing for a positive definite adjoint invariant inner product. Therefore, it is convenient to work with the gauge co-variant derivative since this derivative is compatible with the positive definite inner product on the Lie-algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. A similar strategy on the Einstein part would fail since the associated gauge group would be
SO(3,1)
which is non-compact.

4.3
Sketch of the existence proof

The idea of the proof behind the existence of a solution of the coupled Einstein-Yang-Mills system throughout the domain
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}
given data on the two null hypersurfaces relies on Rendall’s theorem
[
30
]
and standard energy argument for the Cauchy problem. In doing so, we would utilize the estimates on the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvature. We have given the characteristic data on the two null hypersurfaces
H_{0}
and
\underline{H}_{0}
for a quasi-linear wave equation, Rendall’s theorem guarantees the existence of a unique solution in a sufficiently small neighborhood of the topological
2-
sphere
S_{0,0}:=H_{0}\cap\underline{H}_{0}
. To be more precise, we state Rendall’s theorem

Theorem 4.2

[
30
]

Let us consider a quasi-linear wave equation

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}(\Phi)\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\Phi=\mathfrak{F}(\Phi,\partial\Phi),

(4.6)

where
g(\Phi)
and
\mathfrak{F}(\Phi,\partial\Phi)
are smooth in their respective arguments with smooth initial data prescribed on the two intersecting initial null hypersurfaces
H_{0}
and
\underline{H}_{0}
. Suppose
H_{0}
and
\underline{H}_{0}
intersect at the topological
2-
sphere
S_{0,0}
and all derivatives of
\Phi
are continuous up to
S_{0,0}
, then there exists a small neighbourhood of
S_{0,0}
in its future such that a unique solution to (
4.6
) exists within this neighbourhood.

Now it is well-known since the work of
[
4
]
that Einstein’s equations reduce to a system of quasi-linear wave equations in the space-time harmonic gauge. Therefore, in the space-time harmonic gauge, Einstein’s equations (or reduced Einstein’s equations to be precise) fall under the category (
4.6
), and Rendall’s theorem applies. The final step is then to confirm that the gauge condition is verified throughout the domain of existence. This once again follows from the fact that the space-time gauge condition
\Gamma^{\mu}=0
(
\Gamma^{\mu}
is suitably contracted Connection coefficients of the spacetime metric
g
) verifies a wave type equation from which preservation of gauge condition follows in a straightforward way (see section 8 of chapter 6 in
[
25
]
). Our aim is to extend Rendall’s theorem to the coupled Einstein-Yang-Mills system. We want to reduce the coupled system to a system of quasi-linear wave equations. Since Yang-Mills curvature appears as the source term in Einstein’s equations and hence does not alter the principal symbol, we shall only focus on the Yang-Mills equations. Yang-Mills equations can be reduced to a symmetric hyperbolic system in the temporal gauge
[
7
]
. For the moment, if we consider the metric in the form (suitable for the Cauchy problem)

~{}^{1+3}g=-N^{2}dt\otimes dt+g_{ij}dx^{i}\otimes dx^{j}
, where
\{x^{i}\}_{i=0}^{3}=(t,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})
is local chart,
N
is the lapse function. Also, let
\mathbf{n}:=\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}
be the
t=
constant hypersurface orthogonal future directed unit normal field. In the framework of a Cauchy problem, the gauge covariant Yang-Mills equations read
5
5

5

These coupled equations are essentially the first-order formulation of the semi-linear wave equation for the Yang-Mills curvature

\widehat{D}^{\alpha}\widehat{D}_{\alpha}F^{\hat{a}}~_{\hat{b}\mu\nu}=2F^{\hat{a}}~_{\hat{c}\mu\beta}F^{\hat{c}}~_{\hat{b}\nu}~{}^{\beta}-2F^{\hat{a}}~_{\hat{c}\nu\beta}F^{\hat{c}}~_{\hat{b}\mu}~{}^{\beta}-R^{\gamma}~_{\beta\mu\nu}F^{\hat{a}}~_{\hat{b}\gamma}~{}^{\beta}-R^{\gamma}~_{\nu}F^{\hat{a}}~_{\hat{b}\gamma\mu}-R^{\gamma}~_{\mu}F^{\hat{a}}~_{\hat{b}\nu\gamma}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{{\partial}_{t}}\mathcal{E}_{i}=-N\epsilon_{i}~^{jk}\widehat{\nabla}_{j}H_{k}-2NK_{i}~^{j}\mathcal{E}_{j}+Ntr_{g}K\mathcal{E}_{i}-\epsilon_{i}~^{jk}\nabla_{j}NH_{k},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{{\partial}_{t}}H_{i}=N\epsilon_{i}~^{jk}\widehat{\nabla}_{j}\mathcal{E}_{k}-2NK_{i}~^{j}H_{j}+Ntr_{g}KH_{i}+\epsilon_{i}~^{jk}\nabla_{j}N\mathcal{E}_{k},

(4.8)

where
\mathcal{E}_{i}:=F(\mathbf{n},\partial_{i}),H_{i}=~^{*}F(\mathbf{n},e_{i})
are the chromo-electric and chromo-magnetic fields, respectively. Here
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}
denotes the gauge-covariant Lie derivative operator. More explicitly,
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\partial_{t}}:=\partial_{t}+[A_{0},\cdot]
. These system of equations are symmetric hyperbolic and one may obtain a local well-posedness result in the temporal gauge (
A_{0}=0
and the spatial connections
A_{i}
verify
A_{i}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}N\mathcal{E}_{i}dt^{{}^{\prime}}
). Therefore, in the spacetime-harmonic-temporal gauge, the complete Einstein-Yang-Mills equations reduce to coupled quasi-linear-semi-linear wave equations (while Einstein’s equations are quasi-linear, Yang-Mills equations are only semi-linear) for the spacetime metric and the Yang-Mills curvature. An application of Renadall’s theorem
[
30
]
then yields a unique solution in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
S_{0,0}
lying in the causal future of
S_{0,0}

Theorem 4.3

[
30
]

Given a regular initial data set, there exists a small neighborhood to the future of
S_{0,0}
such that Einstein-Yang-Mills equations can be solved within it.

The remaining task is to verify that the gauge conditions and constraints are propagated throughout the domain of existence. This is a rather straightforward calculation and as such a consequence of the Bianchi identities (see
[
25
]
). This is rather monotonous and therefore we do not proceed with the calculations here. Note that there are other gauges that would work equally well. The Spacetime Harmonic Lorentz gauge (SHL) is one such gauge choice among many others. In the SHL gauge, the complete system reduces to a coupled semi-linear wave equations for the connections (see Yvonne Choquet Bruhat’s book
[
25
]
for the local well-posedness of Einstein-Yang-Mills equations in this gauge)

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}A_{\alpha}=\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}(A,\partial A),

(4.9)

where
\partial A
appears linearly in
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha}
. Therefore, Rendall’s theorem directly applies as well. Therefore, at this point, we are able to apply Rendall’s theorem to the Einstein-Yang-Mills system both in spacetime harmonic temporal and spacetime harmonic Lorentz gauge.

Now we sketch the existence proof by utilizing the apriori estimates on the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvature together with Rendall’s theorem. For zero Yang-Mills fields, Luk
[
11
]
sketched an argument for the existence theorem. We follow
[
11
]
. Let us consider the spacetime domain
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}:\{u\leq\epsilon,\underline{u}\leq J\}
and define a time function
t:=u+\underline{u}
. Now by virtue of the null characteristic of
L
and
\underline{L}
,
\nabla t
is time-like. Denote the level sets of
t
by
\Sigma_{t}
. By the theorem of
[
30
]
, if the spacetime does not exist in the whole of
D_{u,\underline{u}}
as a solution of the coupled Einstein-Yang-Mills equations, then there exists a
t^{*}\in(0,J+\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle t^{*}=\sup\{t|\text{the~spacetime~exists~in}~D_{u,\underline{u}}\cap\cup_{\tau\in[0,t)}\Sigma_{\tau}\}.

(4.10)

Now on each Cauchy slice
\Sigma_{t}
, let
\widehat{g}_{t},\widehat{k}_{t},\mathcal{E}_{t},H_{t}
be the induced Riemannian metric, second fundamental form, the chromo-electric field, and the chromo-magnetic field, respectively. We shall argue that they converge to
(\widehat{g}_{t^{*}},\widehat{k}_{t^{*}},\mathcal{E}_{t^{*}},H_{t^{*}})
in a smooth way and
(\widehat{g}_{t^{*}},\widehat{k}_{t^{*}},\mathcal{E}_{t^{*}},H_{t^{*}})
verify the Einstien-Yang-Mills constraint equations. To this end, it suffices to show that all the derivatives of the metric
g
and the Yang-Mills curvatures are uniformly bounded for
t<t^{*}
. Since the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvatures exhaust all the degrees of freedom, we want to control
||\nabla^{I_{1}}_{4}\nabla^{J_{1}}_{3}\nabla^{K_{1}}\Psi||_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{t})}
and
||\widehat{\nabla}^{I_{2}}_{4}\widehat{\nabla}^{J_{2}}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}^{K_{2}}\Phi||_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{t})}
for all
I_{1,2},J_{1,2},
and
K_{1,2}
, where
\Psi=\{\alpha,\beta,\underline{\beta},\rho,\sigma,\underline{\alpha}\}
,
\Phi:\{\alpha^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F},\underline{\alpha}^{F}\}
. This is a consequence of the apriori estimate (the main estimates) that we obtain section 7 and 8. In other words, we have

\displaystyle||\nabla^{I_{1}}_{4}\nabla^{J_{1}}_{3}\nabla^{K_{1}}\Psi||_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{t})}\leq C_{I_{1},J_{1},K_{1}},~||\widehat{\nabla}^{I_{2}}_{4}\widehat{\nabla}^{J_{2}}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}^{K_{2}}\Phi||_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{t})}\leq C_{I_{2},J_{2},K_{2}}~\forall t<t^{*}

(4.11)

and
C_{I_{1},J_{1},K_{1}},C_{I_{2},J_{2},K_{2}}
are independent of
t^{*}
. This can be proven in an iterative manner. Now let us construct spacetime harmonic coordinates on
\Sigma_{t^{*}}
. Let
g_{ij}=(\widehat{g}_{t^{*}})_{ij}
,
g_{00}=-1,g_{0i}=0
and
\partial_{t}g_{ij}=-2k_{ij}
,
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
, and use the freedom on
\partial_{t}g_{0i}
and
\partial_{t}g_{00}
to satisfy
\Gamma^{\mu}:=g^{\alpha\beta}\Gamma[g]^{\mu}_{\alpha\beta}=0
. Similarly, since the Yang-Mills estimates are gauge invariant, we may set
A_{0}=0
for temporal gauge or
\mathfrak{A}:=\partial_{\mu}A^{\mu}=0
for Lorentz gauge. Due to the constraint equations on
\Sigma_{t^{*}}
that are satisfied by continuity, we have
\partial_{t}\Gamma^{\mu}=0
,
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{A}=0
on
\Sigma_{t^{*}}
as well. Now, from the theory of quasi-linear hyperbolic equation, it is straightforward to show that there exists a time
T>t^{*}
such that a unique solution of the coupled Einstein-Yang-Mills system exists in the time slab
t\in[t^{*},T]
. Since the gauge and constraints verify wave equations, if they are zero initially with zero speed, they continue to be zero in the domain of existence. The size of the future domain of existence
T-t^{*}
depends on the size of the data on
\Sigma_{t^{*}}
which by means of the estimates (
4.11
) (obtained due to the estimates in sections 7 and 8) is bounded from above uniformly. Therefore,
T-t^{*}
is strictly greater than zero. This violates the maximality of the domain of existence and therefore
t^{*}=J+\epsilon
i.e., the domain within which we proved the apriori estimates. Now the question remains how to perform a smooth change of coordinate from the spacetime harmonic gauge to the canonical double-null gauge.

In order to smoothly change the coordinates from the spacetime harmonic coordinates to the canonical double null coordinate, we solve the Eikonal equations

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}u\partial_{\nu}u=0,~g^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\underline{u}\partial_{\nu}\underline{u}=0

(4.12)

and the transport equation for the angular variable on topological
2-
sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta^{A}}{\partial\underline{u}}=0,~A=\{1,2\}.

(4.13)

These equations can be solved in the neighbourhood of
\Sigma_{t^{*}}
and doing so one obtains a smooth solution
(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
. By uniqueness, these solutions agree with the canonical double null coordinate functions in a neighborhood to the past of
\Sigma_{t^{*}}
. We can then change to
the
(u,\underline{u},\theta^{1},\theta^{2})
coordinates. This completes the sketch of the existence proof.

5
Important inequalities

Throughout our analysis, we need to employ Sobolev inequalities on the topological 2-sphere
S
at different stages. However, since the metric on
S
is dynamical, we need to make appropriate bootstrap assumptions. Technically, one could define the norms and energies with respect to a background metric on
S
and try to control the additional terms that arise. However, we will start with making a bootstrap assumption on the connection coefficients similar to
[
11
,
6
]
. Let us assume the following

\displaystyle\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\Delta,

(5.1)

where
\varphi:=(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi},\eta,\underline{\omega})
and
\Delta
is possibly a large constant. Later, we will prove that one can choose
\Delta
such that the bootstrap assumption (
5.1
) is closed, in particular, the improved estimates do not depend on
\Delta
. Under this assumption, it is straightforward to prove that the null lapse function
\Omega
, the ellipticity constant of the dynamical metric
\gamma
, and the shift vector field
b^{A}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{A}}
are bounded in the spacetime slab
D_{u,\bar{u}}
(
u\in[0,\epsilon],~\underline{u}\in[0,J]
) in terms of the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
(see
[
6
,
11
]
). For example, consider the null-lapse function
\Omega
. It verifies the following equation by the definition of the connection coefficient
\underline{\omega}

\displaystyle\underline{\omega}=-\frac{1}{2}\Omega^{-1}\nabla_{3}\Omega=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\Omega^{-1}}{du}

(5.2)

which upon integration yields

\displaystyle||\Omega^{-1}-2||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\int_{0}^{u}||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}({S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}}})}\lesssim\epsilon\delta

(5.3)

which implies for sufficiently small
\epsilon
,
\frac{1}{4}\leq\Omega\leq 4
. Similarly one may estimate the metric coefficients
\gamma_{AB}
induced on the
2-
sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}
. These estimates provide us with estimates on the area of
S_{u,\underline{u}}
(
[
6
,
11
]
). Once we have the metric components under control in this double null gauge, we may write down the following set of inequalities that will be useful throughout.

Proposition 5.1

There exists
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\Delta)
such that the following gauge invariant Sobolev inequalities hold for any horizontal gauge field strength in the spacetime slab
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}
(
u\in[0,\epsilon],\underline{u}\in[0,J]
),
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}}})\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\sum_{I=0}^{1}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})},

(5.4)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}),

(5.5)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}}})\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\sum_{I=0}^{2}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(5.6)

Proof.
Let
f=\left(\mathcal{G}^{P}~_{QA_{1}A_{2}\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot A_{n}}\mathcal{G}^{Q}~_{PB_{1}B_{2}\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot B_{n}}\gamma^{A_{1}B_{1}}\gamma^{A_{2}B_{2}}\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot\gamma^{A_{n}B_{n}}+\delta\right)^{1/2}
and apply the standard Sobolev embedding for scalars

\displaystyle||f||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||f||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\frac{\langle\mathcal{G},\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}\rangle}{(|\mathcal{G}|^{2}+\delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}}||_{L^{2}(S)}\right)\leq C\left(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(S)}\right)

after letting
\delta\to 0
.
Note that
\nabla f^{2}=2\mathcal{G}^{P}~_{QA_{1}A_{2}\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot A_{n}}\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}^{Q}~_{PB_{1}B_{2}\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot B_{n}}\gamma^{A_{1}B_{1}}\gamma^{A_{1}B_{1}}\cdot\cdot\cdot\cdot\gamma^{A_{n}B_{n}}
since
f
is gauge invariant and
\widehat{\nabla}
is metrics compatible. The second inequality follows in a similar way. The last inequality is a consequence of the first two.

\square

Proposition 5.2

Given
\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\mbox{tr}\chi,\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})
, the following inequalities hold throughout
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}
(
u\in[0,\epsilon],\underline{u}\in[0,J]
) for a sufficiently small
\epsilon

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}})}+\int_{\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(5.7)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{p}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{p}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{p}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime\prime}},\underline{u})}}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(5.8)

for
1\leq p\leq\infty
. Here
\mathcal{G}
can be a section of the mixed bundle and
\widehat{\nabla}
is the gauge covariant connection compatible with the metrics of both fibers.

Proof.

Follows in an exactly similar way as that of the first one
5.1
.

\square

Proposition 5.3

The following inequalities hold for any horizontal gauge field strength
\mathcal{G}
throughout
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}
(
u\in[0,\epsilon],\underline{u}\in[0,J]
under the bootstrap assumption (
5.1
)) (which in turn controls the metric on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\mathcal{G}||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}||\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\right),

(5.9)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\mathcal{G}||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}||\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\right).

(5.10)

Proof.

Similar to the proof of the proposition
5.1
.

\square

Proposition 5.4

Under the bootstrap assumption (
5.1
), following holds for any horizontal gauge field strength
\mathcal{G}
throughout
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}
(
u\in[0,\epsilon],\underline{u}\in[0,J]
)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\mathcal{G}||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\mathcal{G}||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(H)}(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(H)})^{1/4}\right),

(5.11)

\displaystyle||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\mathcal{G}||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\mathcal{G}||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}(||\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\widehat{\nabla}\mathcal{G}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right).

(5.12)

Proof.

Similar to the proof of the proposition
5.1
.

\square

All of these inequalities hold true for sections of tangent bundles of
S_{u,\underline{u}}
(i.e., the non-gauge field strengths) and in such case,
\widehat{\nabla}
and
\nabla
coincide. These will be the main inequalities that we will use throughout.

6
Estimate of the connection and curvature components in terms of the initial data and curvature energy

We divide the connection coefficients into two classes depending on the transport equations they satisfy. Notice that each element of the set
(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi})
satisfies a
\nabla_{3}
equation, where as
\eta
and
\underline{\omega}
only satisfy a
\nabla_{4}
equation. We denote each element of
(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi})
by
\varphi_{g}
and an element of
(\eta,\underline{\omega})
by
\varphi_{b}
and element of the whole set
(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi},\eta,\underline{\omega})
by
\varphi
. By
C
, we will always mean a universal constant while a constant that depends on the other entities will be denoted so. We first define the curvature norms (both Weyl and Yang-Mills) that we intend to estimate

Lemma 6.1

Assume that
||\nabla^{2}\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})},||\nabla^{2}\varphi_{b}||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S)<\infty
. Then the connection coefficients satisfy the following point-wise estimates

\displaystyle\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0},~\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\varphi_{b}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}).

(6.1)

Proof.

The proof relies on the direct transport inequalities (proposition
5.2
)

\displaystyle||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}})}+\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}}^{\underline{u}}|\nabla_{4}\varphi|d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(6.2)

\displaystyle||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}|\nabla_{3}\varphi|du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(6.3)

and delicate trace estimates. First, assume the bootstrap assumption of the good connection coefficients
\varphi_{g}

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,\epsilon],\underline{u}\in[0,J]}||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq 2C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.4)

Using the transport equations and trace estimates, we will improve this bootstrap estimate thereby completing the proof. First consider the bad connection coefficients
\varphi_{g}:=(\eta,\underline{\omega})
6
6

6

note that one may obtain an equation for
\nabla_{3}\eta
by means of the relation
\nabla_{3}\eta=-\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}+2\nabla_{3}\nabla\ln\Omega

\displaystyle||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,0})}+C\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}|-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\beta-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha^{F}\rho^{F}-\alpha^{F}\sigma^{F})|d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}

(6.5)

which under the boot-strap, Sobolev embedding (
5.6
), and the definition of the entity
\mathcal{F}(S)
yields

\displaystyle||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,0})}+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}+C\mathcal{F}(S)+C\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}|\beta|^{2}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(6.6)

Now recall that
\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}|\beta|^{2}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}
is nothing but the trace norm
||\beta||_{tr(H)}
defined in (
[
6
]
). We may estimate this term by means of the following trace inequality (
[
6
]
)

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}A||_{tr(H)}\leq\left(||\nabla^{2}_{4}A||_{L^{2}(H)}+||A||_{L^{2}(H)}\right)^{1/2}||\nabla^{2}A||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla_{4}\nabla A||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla A||_{L^{2}(H)}

(6.7)

for a section
A
of
TS_{u,\underline{u}}
. Now in order to estimate
||\beta||_{tr(H)}
, we write the following

\displaystyle\beta=-\nabla_{4}\eta\underbrace{-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha^{F}\rho^{F}-\alpha^{F}\sigma^{F})}_{I}.

(6.8)

The term
I
is harmless and its tr norm can be estimated by
C(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))
. Therefore we focus on the
||\cdot||_{tr(H)}
of
\nabla_{4}\eta
. The trace inequality (
6.7
) yields

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\eta||_{tr(H)}\leq\left(||\nabla^{2}_{4}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla_{4}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\right)^{1/2}||\nabla^{2}\eta||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}.

Each term on the right-hand side may be estimated as follows. Using the equation of motion,
\nabla_{4}\eta
is estimated by the curvature norm

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.9)

In order to estimate
||\nabla^{2}_{4}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}
, we act
\nabla_{4}
on the transport equation for
\eta

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{4}\eta=-(\nabla_{4}\widehat{\chi}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{4}\mbox{tr}\chi\gamma)\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\chi\cdot(\nabla_{4}\eta-\underbrace{\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}}_{II})-\nabla_{4}\beta-\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{\nabla_{4}\alpha^{F}}_{III}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})

(6.10)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{F}(\nabla_{4}\rho^{F}-\nabla_{4}\sigma^{F})).

Now notice the terms
II
and
III
do not have explicit expressions in terms of the transport equations. However, term
III
can be estimated by
\mathcal{F}
and
(\eta-\underline{\eta})
verifies
\nabla_{4}
transport equations. Using the evolution equations for
\widehat{\chi}
,
\mbox{tr}\chi
, and
\beta
we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{4}\eta_{a}=-\left((-\mbox{tr}\chi\widehat{\chi}-2\omega\widehat{\chi}-\alpha+\frac{1}{2}(-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\chi)^{2}-|\widehat{\chi}|^{2}_{\gamma}-2\omega\mbox{tr}\chi-\mathfrak{T}_{44})\gamma)\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})\right)_{a}

(6.11)

\displaystyle-\underbrace{(\chi\cdot(\nabla\omega+\frac{1}{2}\chi\cdot(\underline{\eta}-\zeta)+\omega(\underline{\eta}+\zeta)-\frac{1}{2}\beta-\frac{1}{2}~^{*}\sigma^{F}\cdot\alpha^{F}-\frac{1}{2}\rho^{F}\cdot\alpha^{F})_{a}}_{V}

\displaystyle-(-2\mbox{tr}\chi\beta_{a}+(div\alpha)_{a}-2\omega\beta_{a}+(\eta\cdot\alpha)_{a}-\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{\mathcal{D}_{a}R_{44}-\mathcal{D}_{4}R_{4a}}_{IV}))-\frac{1}{2}(\underbrace{\nabla_{4}\alpha^{F}_{a}}_{VI}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+\alpha^{F}_{a}(-\hat{div}\alpha^{F}-\mbox{tr}\chi\rho^{F}-(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\alpha^{F}-(-\hat{curl}\alpha^{F}-\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\alpha^{F})).

Explicit computation using Einstein’s equation with Yang-Mills source i.e.,
R_{\mu\nu}=\mathfrak{T}_{\mu\nu}
yields

\displaystyle IV=\langle\alpha^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{b}\alpha^{F}\rangle-\chi_{bc}\mathfrak{T}_{c4}+\eta_{b}\mathfrak{T}_{44}-2\omega\mathfrak{T}_{4b}-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}(\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot\rho^{F}-\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot\sigma^{F})

(6.12)

and therefore one more use of the evolution equation confirms that
||IV||_{L^{2}(H)}
is controlled by
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. Similarly
||V||_{L^{2}(H)}
and
||VI||_{L^{2}(H)}
are controlled by
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{F}
, and
\mathcal{O}_{0}
under the bootstrap assumption (
6.4
). Luckily we have a
\nabla_{4}
equation for
\eta-\underline{\eta}
(the term
V
arises due to
\nabla_{4}
acting on
\eta-\underline{\eta}
). Also note that
||\Phi^{F}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}:=||(\alpha^{F},\bar{\alpha}^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{\infty}(S))}
can be controlled by
||\Phi^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}
and
||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}
or equivalently
\mathcal{F}(S)
. Collecting all the terms and applying Sobolev embedding, we have

\displaystyle\left(||\nabla^{2}_{4}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla_{4}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\right)^{1/2}||\nabla^{2}\eta||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}),

(6.13)

where
\varphi
can be any element of the set
(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi},\eta,\underline{\omega})
.
The remaining is to estimate
||\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}
. We can do this by means of the evolution equation for
\eta
. Commuting the evolution equation for
\eta
with the horizontal derivative
\nabla
yields (schematically)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta=-\nabla\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\chi\cdot(\nabla\eta-\nabla\underline{\eta})-\nabla\beta-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}(\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F})

(6.14)

\displaystyle+(\beta+\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\alpha^{F})\eta+(\eta+\underline{\eta})(-\chi(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\beta-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))-\chi\nabla\eta+\chi\underline{\eta}\eta

which utilizing Sobolev embedding on
S_{u,\underline{u}}
(to handle the term
||\eta||_{L^{4}(S_{u,v})}
) can be estimated as

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}).

(6.15)

Collecting all the terms together, we obtain

\displaystyle||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,0})}+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\eta||_{L^{\infty}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(6.16)

which upon using Grönwall yields

\displaystyle||\eta||_{L^{\infty}}(S_{u,\underline{u}})\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}).

(6.17)

Later, we shall see that
\mathcal{F}(S)
is actually dominated by
\mathcal{F}
and
||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}
is dominated by
\mathcal{W}
. Now we want to estimate
\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
. Once again, the use of transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) yields

\displaystyle||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}})}+\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}|2\omega\underline{\omega}+\frac{3}{4}|\eta-\underline{\eta}|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot(\eta+\underline{\eta})\right.

(6.18)

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{8}|\eta+\underline{\eta}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{T}_{43}|d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right).

Once again, we observe that all the terms except
\rho
enjoy estimates that are controlled by
\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}
, and
\mathcal{F}(S)
. Therefore, we focus on the term
\rho
. The previous inequality reduces to

\displaystyle||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq

(6.19)

\displaystyle C||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}})}+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}(S))+C\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\rho^{2}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now notice
\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\rho^{2}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}=||\rho||_{tr(H)}
. Now we follow the same procedure as before i.e., utilize the trace inequality (
6.7
). Since
\omega
does not satisfy a
\nabla_{4}
equation, we want to get rid of
\omega
. Write
\rho
as follows after the re-scaling
\underline{\omega}=\Omega\tilde{\underline{\omega}}

\displaystyle\rho=\Omega\nabla_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}-\underbrace{\frac{3}{4}|\eta+\underline{\eta}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot(\eta+\underline{\eta})+\frac{1}{8}|\eta+\underline{\eta}|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}((\rho^{F})^{2}+(\sigma^{F})^{2})}_{V}.

Here the term
V
is harmless following the bootstrap (
6.4
) and the previous estimate (
6.17
). Therefore, we only focus on the term
\Omega\nabla_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}
and noting
||\Omega||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})
under bootstrap, estimating
||\nabla_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{tr(H)}
suffices. Now we use the trace inequality to estimate
||\nabla_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{tr(H)}

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{tr(H)}\leq\left(||\nabla^{2}_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla_{4}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{L^{2}(H)}\right)^{1/2}||\nabla^{2}\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla_{4}\nabla\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla\tilde{\underline{\omega}}||_{L^{2}(H)}.

Following the same procedure as before and controlling
||\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(H)}
by
||\nabla^{2}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(H)}
and
\mathcal{W}
. Collecting all the terms together, one obtains

\displaystyle||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,0})}+\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

which upon utilizing Grönwall’s inequality yields

\displaystyle||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}).

(6.20)

Now we estimate the good connection coefficients
\varphi_{g}
i.e., the ones satisfying
\nabla_{3}
equations. We use transport inequality (proposition
5.2
). First consider
\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}
and use that fact that
||\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}
is controlled by
\mathcal{F}
together with Sobolev embedding (
5.6
)

\displaystyle||\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq

(6.21)

\displaystyle\frac{C}{2}\left(||\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||-\frac{1}{2}(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})^{2}-|\underline{\widehat{\chi}}|^{2}_{\gamma}-2\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-\mathfrak{T}_{33}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}

if we choose
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small. Now consider
\underline{\widehat{\chi}}

\displaystyle||\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{C}{2}\left(||\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\int_{0}^{u}|\nabla_{3}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}|du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

(6.22)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\frac{C}{2}\int_{0}^{u}||-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}-2\underline{\omega}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}+\frac{C}{2}\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\bar{\alpha}^{2}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Now we need to estimate
\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\bar{\alpha}^{2}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}=||\bar{\alpha}||_{tr(\underline{H})}
. Proceeding exactly the similar way as before i.e., write
\alpha
as follows

\displaystyle\bar{\alpha}=-\Omega\nabla_{3}\tilde{\underline{\widehat{\chi}}}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}

(6.23)

where
\tilde{\underline{\widehat{\chi}}}=\frac{1}{\Omega}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}
since we do not have a
\nabla_{3}
equation for
\underline{\omega}
and
||\Omega||_{L^{\infty}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})
under the bootstrap assumption
5.1
. Proceeding exactly the same way and collecting all the terms we obtain

\displaystyle||\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}(\underline{\eta},\eta)||_{L^{2}(H)})

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}(\underline{\eta},\eta)||_{L^{2}(H)})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathcal{O}_{0}

after choosing sufficiently small
\epsilon
. Since in the trace estimate we need
||\nabla^{2}_{3}\tilde{\underline{\widehat{\chi}}}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
, we will need
||\nabla_{3}\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
and therefore we include
||\nabla_{3}\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
in the definition of
\mathcal{W}
from the beginning. Exact similar procedure yields

\displaystyle||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.24)

Now for
\mbox{tr}\chi
and
\widehat{\chi}
, we will encounter terms that can be estimated by
||\nabla^{2}(\eta,\underline{\eta})||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}
, and
\mathcal{F}(S)
. The transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) yields

\displaystyle||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+C\int_{0}^{u}||-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2|\eta|^{2}-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}

\displaystyle+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{u}(\nabla\eta)^{2}du^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{u}\rho^{2}du^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Therefore notice that we need to estimate two terms
\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{u}(\nabla\eta)^{2}du^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}=||\nabla\eta||_{tr(\underline{H})}
and
\sup_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}(\int_{0}^{u}\rho^{2}du^{{}^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}:=||\rho||_{tr(\underline{H})}
. We have the following estimate

\displaystyle||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}||\nabla\eta||_{tr(\underline{H})}+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}||\rho||_{tr(\underline{H})}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.25)

Similarly, for
\widehat{\chi}
, we obtain through the use of the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
)

\displaystyle||\widehat{\chi}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+C\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}||\nabla\eta||_{tr(\underline{H})}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0},

(6.26)

where we can estimate
||\rho||_{tr(\underline{H})}
and
||\nabla\eta||_{tr(\underline{H})}
in terms of
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
and
(\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})
in a similar way as the other entities such as
\underline{\omega}
and
\eta
and therefore we omit the calculations. The most important point to note here is the presence of a smallness factor
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
with
tr(\underline{H})
norm of
\rho
and
\nabla\eta
. This concludes the
L^{\infty}
estimates for the connection coefficients.

\square

Lemma 6.2

Assume
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})}<\infty
. Then
||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}
and
||\varphi_{b}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})})
, where
\varphi_{g}:=(\widehat{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\mbox{tr}\chi,\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\eta},\omega)
and
\varphi_{b}:=(\eta,\underline{\omega})
.

Proof.

We prove it under the assumption

\displaystyle||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.27)

and later try to improve it. We start with
\eta
since it satisfies a
\nabla_{4}
equation. A direct application of the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) yields

\displaystyle||\eta||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\eta||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

(6.28)

\displaystyle=C\left(||\eta||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\beta-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{u}||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}.

An application of Grönwall’s inequality leads to
the desired estimate for
\eta

\displaystyle||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))e^{C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\underline{u}}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.29)

since
\underline{u}\leq J
. Now we repeat the same procedure for
\underline{\omega}
which also satisfies a
\nabla_{4}
transport equation

\displaystyle||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=C\left(||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||2\omega\underline{\omega}+\frac{3}{4}|\eta-\underline{\eta}|^{2}-\frac{1}{4}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot(\eta+\underline{\eta})-\frac{1}{8}|\eta+\underline{\eta}|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\rho+\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})e^{C(\mathcal{O}_{0}J)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}),

where the last step follows from an application of the Grönwall’s inequality and
\underline{u}\leq J
.
Notice an extremely important fact:
||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}
only depends on
\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),
and
\mathcal{F}(S)
, whereas
||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}
depends on
\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}
. This will be vital when we show
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H/\underline{H})}<\infty
given
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S)<\infty
. Now we move on to estimating
\varphi_{g}
i.e., the connection coefficients that satisfy
\nabla_{3}
equation. We start with
\underline{\eta}
. Using the assumption (
6.27
) together with the previous estimate (lemma 6.2) and estimates for
||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}
, we obtain through the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
)

\displaystyle||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq\frac{C}{2}\left(||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}

for sufficiently small
\epsilon
. Estimate for
||\mbox{tr}\chi||
(and similar others involving
\nabla\eta/\underline{\eta}
) follows in a similar way but now we need to use
||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\lesssim||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}
Sobolev embedding to close the estimate. transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) yields

\displaystyle||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq\frac{C}{2}\left(||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{4}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{2}\left(||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2div\eta+2|\eta|^{2}+2\rho-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{4}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}

for sufficiently small
\epsilon
. Through a similar argument, we obtain the improved estimates for the remaining connection coefficients that is we prove

\displaystyle||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.31)

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

\square

These estimates will be extremely crucial in proving the following lemma as well as estimating
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H/\underline{H})}
in terms of
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),
and
\mathcal{F}(S)
in near future (lemma
6.4
). Notice another important point: since
||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
, we have
||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
i.e.,
L^{2}(S)
of
\underline{\omega}
does not depend on
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}
.

Lemma 6.3

Assume
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})}<\infty
. Then
||\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}
and
||\nabla\varphi_{b}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})})
, where
\varphi_{g}:=(\widehat{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\mbox{tr}\chi,\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\eta},\omega)
and
\varphi_{b}:=(\eta,\underline{\omega})
.

Proof.
We will prove this under the assumption

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq 2C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.32)

We will obtain a better estimate, therefore, closing the argument. As usual, we first start with
\eta
since it satisfies a
\nabla_{4}
equation. We commute
\nabla
with the transport equation (
3.24
) satisfied by
\eta
to obtain (we write it in a schematic way)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta=-\nabla\chi(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\chi(\nabla\eta-\nabla\underline{\eta})-\nabla\beta-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}(\nabla\rho^{F}-\nabla\sigma^{F})

(6.33)

\displaystyle+(\beta+\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))\eta+(\eta+\underline{\eta})(-\chi(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\beta-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})).

The transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) for
\nabla\eta
reads

\displaystyle||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\right).

(6.34)

Under the assumption (
6.32
) and the previous estimates (lemma
6.1
-
6.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(6.35)

which after using Grönwall’s inequality yields

\displaystyle||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})e^{C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\underline{u}}\leq

(6.36)

\displaystyle C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})

since
\underline{u}\leq J
.
A similar argument for
\underline{\omega}
yields

\displaystyle||\nabla\underline{\omega}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}).

(6.37)

Now we want to estimate the good connection coefficients i.e., the ones that satisfy
\nabla_{3}
equation. Let us start with
\underline{\eta}
. Commuting the transport equation of
\underline{\eta}
with
\nabla
yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\nabla\underline{\eta}=

(6.38)

\displaystyle-\nabla\bar{\chi}(\underline{\eta}-\eta)-\chi(\nabla\eta-\nabla\underline{\eta})+\nabla\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\nabla\rho^{F}-\nabla\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+(\bar{\beta}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))\underline{\eta}+(\eta+\underline{\eta})(-\bar{\chi}(\underline{\eta}-\eta)+\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})).

Similarly, we may apply the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) for
||\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S)}
to obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq\frac{C}{2}\left(||\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S_{0,u})}+\int_{0}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

(6.39)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{W})

which can be made to satisfy the following estimate after choosing sufficiently small
\epsilon
yields

\displaystyle||\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}.

(6.40)

Now we estimate
\mbox{tr}\chi
. Commuting
\nabla
with the transport equation of
\mbox{tr}\chi
yields the following equation (schematic)

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi=

(6.41)

\displaystyle 2\nabla\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\underline{\omega}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi+2\nabla^{2}\eta+2\eta\nabla\eta+2\nabla\rho-\nabla\widehat{\chi}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}-\widehat{\chi}\nabla\underline{\widehat{\chi}}.

The transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) provides the following estimate for
\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi
under the assumption (
6.32
) together with the previous estimates for
\eta
and
\underline{\omega}

\displaystyle||\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq\frac{C}{2}\left(||\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{2}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||2\nabla\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\underline{\omega}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi+2\nabla^{2}\eta\right.

(6.42)

\displaystyle\left.+2\eta\nabla\eta+2\nabla\rho-\nabla\widehat{\chi}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}-\widehat{\chi}\nabla\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\int_{0}^{u}||\frac{1}{2}\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})},\mathcal{W})\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}

for sufficiently small
\epsilon
. Notice that here we needed
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
or more precisely
||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
. The remaining connection coefficients are estimated in an exactly similar way. We sketch the estimates below

\displaystyle||\nabla\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\frac{C}{2}\left(||\nabla\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\underline{\widehat{\chi}}||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime\prime}},\underline{u})}}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(6.43)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon^{1/2}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S)\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0},

\displaystyle||\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\hat{\underline{\eta}}||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\hat{\underline{\eta}}||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime\prime}},\underline{u})}}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(6.44)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon^{1/2}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S)\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0},

\displaystyle||\nabla\omega||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\omega||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\omega||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime\prime}},\underline{u})}}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(6.45)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon^{1/2}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S)\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0},

\displaystyle||\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime\prime}},\underline{u})}}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon^{1/2}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S)\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0},

\displaystyle||\nabla\widehat{\chi}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\widehat{\chi}||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime}},\underline{u}})}+\int_{u^{{}^{\prime}}}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\widehat{\chi}||_{L^{2}(S_{u^{{}^{\prime\prime}},\underline{u})}}du^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\mathcal{O}_{0}}{2}+\epsilon^{1/2}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S)\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})\leq C\mathcal{O}_{0}

after choosing
\epsilon
sufficiently small.
In this case, we will hit
\mathfrak{T}_{ab}\sim\rho^{F}\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\sigma^{F}\sigma^{F}
by
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
derivative and therefore we must incorporate
||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
in the
\mathcal{F}
norm. This completes the proof of the lemma.

\square

Corollary 6.1

The Gauss curvature
K
of the topological
2
-sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}
satisfies

\displaystyle||K||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

(6.46)

\displaystyle||\nabla K||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}(S))+||\nabla\rho||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}.

(6.47)

Proof:
A direct consequence of the null Hamiltonian constraint (
3.33
), lemma (
6.1
), (
6.2
), (
6.3
), and the definitions of
\mathcal{W}(S)
and
\mathcal{F}(S)
.

Lemma 6.4

Let
\varphi_{g}:=(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi})
and
\varphi_{b}:=(\eta,\underline{\omega})
and
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S)<\infty
. Then
||\nabla^{2}\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
and
||\nabla^{2}\varphi_{b}||_{L^{2}(H,\underline{H})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
.

Proof.

Following
[
6
]
, we prove this lemma by means of constructing a transport-Hodge system.
The basic idea is the following. We construct a set of new entities
\Xi:=\{\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi,\mu,\bar{\xi}\}
and
\bar{\Xi}:=\{\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\bar{\mu},\xi\}
. We first obtain their transport equations. We proceed exactly the same way as
[
6
]
, only keeping track of the additional Yang-Mills curvature terms. Yang-Mills curvature terms are harmless in this context since they have one order higher regularity than the Weyl curvature. We define
\mu,\bar{\mu}
and
\xi,\bar{\xi}
as follows

\displaystyle\mu:=-\text{div}\eta-\rho,~\bar{\mu}:-\text{div}\underline{\eta}-\rho,~\xi:=\nabla\omega+~^{*}\nabla\omega^{{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}\beta,

(6.48)

\displaystyle\bar{\xi}:=-\nabla\underline{\omega}+~^{*}\nabla\underline{\omega}^{{\dagger}}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\beta},

(6.49)

where
\omega^{{\dagger}}
and
\underline{\omega}^{{\dagger}}
are the auxiliary variables that satisfy the following boundary-valued equations

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\omega^{{\dagger}}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma,~\omega^{{\dagger}}=0~on~H_{0},~\nabla_{4}\underline{\omega}^{{\dagger}}=\frac{1}{2}\sigma,~\underline{\omega}^{{\dagger}}=0~on~\underline{H}_{0}.

(6.50)

By definition, we have the Hodge system

{}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{1}\langle\underline{\omega}\rangle=\bar{\xi}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{\beta},~~^{*}\mathcal{D}_{1}\langle\omega\rangle=\xi+\frac{1}{2}\beta,

(6.51)

\displaystyle\text{div}\eta=-\mu-\rho,~\text{curl}\eta=\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\wedge\widehat{\chi}+\sigma,

(6.52)

\displaystyle\text{div}\widehat{\chi}=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi-\frac{1}{2}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot(\widehat{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\delta_{ab})-\beta+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{T}(e_{4},\cdot)

(6.53)

\displaystyle\text{div}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}=\frac{1}{2}\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}(\underline{\eta}-\eta)\cdot(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\delta_{ab})-\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{T}(e_{3},\cdot),

(6.54)

where
{}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{1}\langle\omega\rangle:=\nabla\omega+~^{*}\nabla\omega^{{\dagger}}
and
{}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{1}\langle\underline{\omega}\rangle:=-\nabla\underline{\omega}+~^{*}\nabla\underline{\omega}^{{\dagger}}
.
\Xi=\mu
satisfies the following type of transport equation (schematically)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\mu=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\text{div}(-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta}))}_{A}-\underbrace{\text{div}\beta}_{-ID}-\underbrace{\text{div}(\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))-(\underbrace{-\text{div}\beta}_{ID}-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\rho-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha+\zeta\cdot\beta+2\underline{\eta}\cdot\beta}_{IA}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{1}{4}(\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}-\underline{\omega}|\alpha^{F}|^{2}+4\eta\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})-\rho^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}-2\underline{\eta}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}))}_{IB}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{(\beta+(\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\alpha^{F}))\eta+(\eta+\underline{\eta})(-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\beta-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))}_{IC}-\chi\nabla\eta+\chi\underline{\eta}\eta

where all the terms involving Yang-Mills curvature can be controlled by means of null Yang-Mills equations. The most important point is to note that
\text{div}\beta
terms cancel each other in a point-wise way. This is the purpose of constructing the new function
\mu
(and similar others) so that the derivative of the Weyl curvature does not appear since that would obstruct closing the regularity argument. Now observe an extremely important point: term
A
would contain
\text{div}(\chi)(\eta-\underline{\eta})
and we can need to estimate this term in
L^{2}(S)
in order to estimate
||\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}
using the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
). Notice the following calculations

\displaystyle||A||_{L^{2}(S)}=||-\text{div}(-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta}))||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\chi||_{L^{\infty}(S)}(||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S)})

(6.55)

\displaystyle+||\nabla\chi||_{L^{4}}(||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}+||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S)})

Now since
\widehat{\chi}
and
\mbox{tr}\chi
both satisfy
\nabla_{3}
equation, we may estimate
||\nabla\chi||_{L^{4}(S)}
solely by means of the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
using codimension-1 trace inequality

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq

(6.56)

\displaystyle C\left(||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\nabla\varphi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}(||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\nabla\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right)

given
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}<\infty
. This is because, we gain
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
from the term
||\nabla\varphi||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
. From lemma (
6.2
), we have the estimates for
||\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}
in terms of
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)
and for
||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S)}
in terms of
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. Therefore after choosing
\epsilon
sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle||A||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.57)

Now an application of transport inequality (proposition
5.2
)

\displaystyle||\mu||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\mu||_{L^{2}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right)

(6.58)

yields

\displaystyle||\mu||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(6.59)

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}(||IA,IB,IC||_{L^{2}(S)})d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

and from the Hodge system (
6.52
)

\displaystyle||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})(||K||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\rho||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\sigma||_{L^{2}(S)})+||\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}.

(6.60)

Therefore we are left to estimate
||IA||_{L^{2}(S)},||IB||_{L^{2}(S)},
and
||IC||_{L^{2}(S)}
. Using the null Yang-Mills equations and elementary inequality such as Holder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle||IA||_{L^{2}(S)}+||IB||_{L^{2}(S)}+||IC||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq

(6.61)

\displaystyle C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})(||\alpha||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\beta||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\rho||_{L^{2}(S)})

substitution of which in (
6.59
), an application of Grönwall, and integration in
\underline{u}
yield

\displaystyle||\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.62)

which in turn yields

\displaystyle||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.63)

since
||\alpha||_{L^{2}(S)},||\beta||_{L^{2}(S)},||\rho||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{W}(S))
. In this process, we obtain
||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}
independent of
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H,\bar{H)}}
and therefore improve (
6.36
).

Similarly, analysing the pair
(\bar{\xi},\langle\underline{\omega}\rangle)
, we can estimate
||\nabla\underline{\omega}||_{L^{2}(S)}
by means of

C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
utilizing the estimate for
||\nabla\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}
which is now independent of
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H,\bar{H)}}
. This is the whole point of obtaining estimates in a hierarchical way i.e., start with
\eta
and estimate
\underline{\omega}
by using the estimate for
\eta
and then continue to do so for the remaining connection coefficients.
An extremely important point is that
\bar{\alpha}
does not appear due to the special structure of the null-Bianchi equations (recall
\bar{\alpha}
can not be controlled on
H
). Now we want to estimate
||\nabla^{2}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}
. Recall, we have constructed the entities
\mu,\bar{\mu},\xi,\bar{\xi}
which adding
\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi
and
\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}
constructs the set
\Xi:=\{\nabla\mbox{tr}\chi,\mu,\bar{\xi}\}
and
\bar{\Xi}:=\{\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\bar{\mu},\xi\}
. We obtain a set of following transport equations presented in a schematic way for
\nabla\Xi
and
\nabla\bar{\Xi}

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\nabla\Xi=\varphi\nabla^{2}\varphi+\nabla\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi\nabla\Psi+\Psi\nabla\varphi+\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\Phi^{F}

(6.64)

\displaystyle+\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\varphi\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\Phi^{F}\cdot\Phi^{F}\nabla\varphi

\displaystyle+\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi_{g}\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi\varphi\nabla\varphi_{g}+\varphi_{g}\nabla\Xi+\varphi\nabla_{4}\Xi+\varphi\varphi_{g}\Xi:=\mathcal{F}_{1}

where
\Psi
can consist of any of the ‘good’ Weyl curvature components i.e.,
(\alpha,\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
or more precisely it does not contain
\alpha
. Similarly,
\Phi^{F}
can contain all the Yang-Mills curvature components
(\alpha^{F},\bar{\alpha}^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})
. However, there is no term involving
\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\bar{\alpha}^{F}
i.e., the topmost derivative operator does not act on
\bar{\alpha}^{F}
. This is once again a consequence of the special structure of the Yang-Mills equations. Of course, Yang-Mills equations do satisfy a null condition. In addition, a good
\varphi
(
\varphi_{g}
) always appears multiplied with the top derivative of
\varphi
. Similarly, we obtain the following transport equation for
\bar{\Xi}

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\nabla\bar{\Xi}=\varphi\nabla^{2}\varphi+\nabla\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi\nabla\Psi+\Psi\nabla\varphi+\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\Phi^{F}

(6.65)

\displaystyle+\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\varphi\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\varphi\nabla\varphi

\displaystyle+\Phi^{F}\cdot\Phi^{F}\nabla\varphi+\varphi_{g}\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi\varphi\nabla\varphi_{g}+\varphi_{g}\nabla\bar{\Xi}+\varphi\nabla_{3}\bar{\Xi}+\varphi\varphi_{g}\bar{\Xi}:=\mathcal{F}_{2}.

Similarly,
\Psi
represents the Weyl curvature components belonging to the set
(\bar{\alpha},\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
.
\Phi^{F}
can contain all the Yang-Mills curvature components
(\alpha^{F},\bar{\alpha}^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})
. However, we do not have
\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\alpha^{F}
term. This is favorable to us since we do not have control of
\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\alpha^{F}
on
\underline{H}
. Now we may apply the direct transport inequalities (proposition
5.2
) to obtain estimates for
\nabla\Xi
and
\nabla\bar{\Xi}
. Also notice
\nabla_{4}\Xi
satisfies equation of the following type

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\Xi=\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi\Psi+\Phi^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\varphi\Phi^{F}\Phi^{F}+\varphi_{g}\varphi\varphi+\varphi\varphi,

(6.66)

where
\Psi
contains the Weyl curvature components
(\alpha,\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
i.e., the ones that can be controlled over
H
. Similarly,
\nabla_{3}\bar{\Xi}
satisfies equation of the following type

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\bar{\Xi}=\varphi\nabla\varphi+\nabla\varphi_{g}\varphi+\varphi\Psi+\Phi^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\varphi\Phi^{F}\Phi^{F}+\varphi_{g}\varphi\varphi+\varphi\varphi.

(6.67)

Once again
\Psi
appearing in
\nabla_{3}\bar{\Xi}
equation contains the Weyl curvature components that can be controlled over
\underline{H}
. Remarkably note that equations for
\nabla_{4}\Xi
and
\nabla_{3}\bar{\Xi}
do not contain derivatives of the Weyl curvature. As mentioned previously, this is vital to close the regularity. Also
L^{2}(S)
and
\dot{H}^{1}(S)
of
\varphi_{g}
can be controlled only by means of the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. We utilize the transport inequalities (proposition
5.2
) to estimate
\nabla\Xi
and
\nabla\bar{\Xi}

\displaystyle||\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\left(||\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right),

(6.68)

\displaystyle||\nabla\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{3}\nabla\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\right).

(6.69)

Therefore we will need to estimate
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
in
L^{2}(S)
. We first estimate different elements of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
(
6.64
-
6.65
) using estimates derived in lemma
6.1
-
6.3

\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}
:

\displaystyle||\varphi\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\nabla\varphi||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(||\nabla\varphi||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(S)}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}})^{2}\leq C||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

\displaystyle||\varphi\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)})||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\leq C(||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)})||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})(||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\Psi||^{2}_{L^{2}(S)}).

The remaining terms are estimated as follows

\displaystyle||\Psi\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}

(6.70)

\displaystyle\lesssim||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S)}(||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)})

\displaystyle\lesssim C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)},||\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\leq||\Phi^{F}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{F}(S))||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)},

\displaystyle~||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{F}(S)),||\varphi\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\leq||\varphi||_{L^{4}}||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\lesssim||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\lesssim||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

and

\displaystyle||\varphi\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\Phi^{F}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.71)

\displaystyle(||\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}+||\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}),~||\varphi\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\lesssim||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\lesssim(||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)})

\displaystyle\lesssim C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

\displaystyle||\varphi_{g}\varphi\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\lesssim||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

(6.72)

\displaystyle||\varphi\varphi\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim||\varphi||^{2}_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S)),

\displaystyle||\varphi\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}=||\nabla^{2}\varphi+\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\leq||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)},~||\varphi_{g}\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\leq||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}.

The last type of terms are estimated as follows

\displaystyle||\varphi\nabla_{4}\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}=||\varphi(\varphi\nabla\varphi+\varphi\Psi+\Phi^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}+\varphi\Phi^{F}\Phi^{F}+\varphi_{g}\varphi\varphi+\varphi\varphi)||_{L^{2}(S)}

(6.73)

\displaystyle\lesssim||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}}||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}

\displaystyle+||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}+||\varphi||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}||\Phi^{F}||^{2}_{L^{\infty}(S)}

\displaystyle+||\varphi_{g}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}||\varphi||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S)}+||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}}||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S)}

\displaystyle+||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}}||\varphi||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

\displaystyle||\varphi\nabla_{3}\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

\displaystyle||\varphi\varphi_{g}\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim C||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},~||\varphi\varphi_{g}\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim C||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)},

where we extensively used the inequalities (
5.4
-
5.10
). Notice that we once again proceed in a hierarchical fashion i.e., start
(\mu,\eta)
and then use that result to obtain estimates for
(\bar{\xi},\underline{\omega})
and so on. This is one of the the main reasons why
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
are estimated by means of
C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
and
||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}
(linearly in the latter). Collecting all the terms together, we obtain the following two inequalities satisfied by
\nabla\Xi
and
\nabla\bar{\Xi}

\displaystyle||\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))(1+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}),

(6.74)

\displaystyle||\nabla\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))(1+\int_{0}^{u}||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}})

(6.75)

Now we use the elliptic estimates resulting from the Hodge system.
After an application of
\mathcal{D}^{*}
, the Hodge system (
6.51
-
6.54
) reduces to the following second order elliptic equation

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{*}\mathcal{D}\varphi=K\varphi+\nabla(\Xi/\bar{\Xi})+\nabla\Psi+\varphi\nabla\varphi+\nabla\varphi+\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}

(6.76)

which yields an estimate of the type

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim||K\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla(\Xi/\bar{\Xi})||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\varphi\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle+||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\Phi^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+||\nabla(\Xi/\bar{\Xi})||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}

(6.77)

substitution of which in the previous inequalities (
6.74
-
6.75
) and an application of Grönwall’s inequality yields

\displaystyle||\nabla\Xi||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),~||\nabla\bar{\Xi}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.78)

Substitution of (
6.78
) into the elliptic estimate (
6.77
) yields

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(S)}\lesssim C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)},

(6.79)

for
\varphi\in(\widehat{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\mbox{tr}\chi,\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\eta,\underline{\eta},\omega,\underline{\omega})
and the most important point is that
\Psi
in the elliptic estimate (
6.79
) does not contain
\alpha,\bar{\alpha}
. Therefore integrating over
H
and
\underline{H}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)},||\nabla^{2}\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.80)

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

\square

Corollary 6.2

||\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0}),~||\nabla\varphi_{b}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
.

Proof.
The proof relies on the co-dimension 1 trace inequalities

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\nabla_{4}\nabla\varphi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(H)}(||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\nabla\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})^{1/4}\right),

(6.81)

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\nabla\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\nabla\varphi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}(||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\nabla\nabla\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right),

(6.82)

the null evolution equations and lemma (4) and (5). For the good connection coefficients
\varphi_{g}
i.e., the ones satisfying
\nabla_{3}
equations, we easily observe the following using lemma (4)

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi_{g}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}=\int_{0}^{u}||\nabla\varphi_{g}||^{2}_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0}).

(6.83)

In addition using the commuted null transport equation,
||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H}}
is controlled by
||\nabla^{2}\varphi_{g}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
which in turn is controlled by
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
by lemma (5). For example, if we look at the commuted
\nabla_{3}
equation for
\widehat{\chi}

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\nabla\widehat{\chi}\sim-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\widehat{\chi}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\nabla\widehat{\chi}+\nabla^{2}\eta+2\nabla(\underline{\omega}\widehat{\chi})-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\underline{\widehat{\chi}})

(6.84)

\displaystyle+\eta\nabla\eta+\nabla(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+(\bar{\beta}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}))\widehat{\chi}+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\nabla_{3}\widehat{\chi}-\bar{\chi}\nabla\widehat{\chi}+\bar{\chi}\eta\widehat{\chi},

we observe every term at the right-hand side can be estimated in
L^{2}(\underline{H})
by
C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
. Similar results hold for other good connection coefficients. Therefore we gain an overall factor of
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
arising from the integration over
\underline{H}
i.e.,

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.85)

which for sufficiently small
\epsilon
yields

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi_{g}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0}).

(6.86)

Now for the bad connection coefficients
\varphi_{g}
that is the ones satisfying
\nabla_{4}
equations, we would not have
||\nabla\varphi_{b}||_{L^{4}(S)}
determined solely in terms of the initial data rather by

C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))
. This is because we do not gain a factor
\epsilon
from the integral over
H
. Therefore putting everything together, using lemma (4) and (5) along with the
\nabla
commuted
\nabla_{4}
equations for
\varphi_{g}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla\varphi_{b}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.87)

This completes the proof.

\square

In the spirit of the previous lemma, we will prove a similar estimate for mixed derivatives of connection coefficients that can not be estimated directly using their evolution equations. We need to set up a transport-div-curl system. More precisely, we need to utilize transport inequalities (proposition
5.2
) together with the elliptic estimates. We do so in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.5

Let
\varphi_{g}:=(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\underline{\eta},\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi,\widehat{\chi})
and
\varphi_{b}:=(\eta,\underline{\omega})
and
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S)<\infty
. Then
||\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)},||\nabla\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}||_{L^{2}(H)},~||\nabla\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})},||\nabla\nabla_{4}\omega||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

Proof.
The proof follows in a similar fashion as that of
6.4
. Given the estimates obtained in the previous lemmas, we will only prove it for one connection coefficient
\eta
. The remaining estimates can be obtained in an exactly similar fashion. Let us recall the Hodge transport system for the pair
(\eta,\mu)
(schematically)

\displaystyle\text{div}\eta=-\mu-\rho,~\text{curl}\eta=\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\wedge\widehat{\chi}+\sigma

(6.88)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\mu=-\text{div}(-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta}))-\text{div}(\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))-(-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\rho-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha+\zeta\cdot\beta+2\underline{\eta}\cdot\beta
