\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}-\underline{\omega}|\alpha^{F}|^{2}+4\eta\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})-\rho^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}-2\underline{\eta}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}))

\displaystyle+((\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\alpha^{F}))\eta+(\eta+\underline{\eta})(-\chi\cdot(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))-\underline{\eta}\beta-\chi\nabla\eta+\chi\underline{\eta}\eta.

If we commute the transport equation for
\mu
with
\nabla_{3}
we obtain an equation of the following type (we keep the potentially dangerous terms in exact form and write the harmless terms in a schematic way)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\mu\sim\chi\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta+\underbrace{\nabla\chi\nabla_{3}\eta}_{IIA}+\widehat{\text{div}}(\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F})\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})-\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F})}_{IIC1}

\displaystyle+\widehat{\text{div}}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\text{div}}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\mbox{tr}\chi\nabla_{3}\rho+\rho\nabla_{3}\mbox{tr}\chi+\nabla_{3}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\alpha+\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\nabla_{3}\alpha

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\beta\nabla_{3}\zeta}_{IIB}+\zeta\nabla_{3}\beta+\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}\beta+\underline{\eta}\nabla_{3}\beta+\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}^{2}_{3}\alpha^{F}}_{IIC2}+\underbrace{\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}|\alpha^{F}|^{2}}+\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\nabla_{3}\eta\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}}_{IID}-\sigma^{F})+\eta\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\alpha^{F})\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\eta\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}+\underbrace{\rho^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}}_{IIE}

\displaystyle+\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}+\underbrace{\sigma^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}}_{IIF}+\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+\underline{\eta}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\alpha^{F})\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+\underline{\eta}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\chi\eta\nabla_{3}\eta}_{IIG}+\chi\underline{\eta}\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}+(|\eta|^{2}+|\underline{\eta}|^{2})\nabla_{3}\chi+\eta\underline{\eta}\nabla_{3}\chi+\chi\eta\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta}+\underbrace{\chi\underline{\eta}\nabla_{3}\eta}_{IIH}+\underbrace{\nabla_{3}\chi\nabla\eta}_{IIK}

\displaystyle+\omega\nabla_{3}\mu+\underline{\omega}\nabla_{4}\mu+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\nabla\mu.

Let us identify the terms that do need care. The term
IIC1
is extremely dangerous since
\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}
and
\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}
contain terms of the type
\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\bar{\alpha}^{F}
which can not be controlled on
H
. Now, the previous equation is a
\nabla_{4}
transport equation for
\nabla_{3}\mu
and therefore after using the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
),
|\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}
gets integrated over
H
which is absolutely not under control. However, we also do have the term
IIC2
which contains terms that cancel the dangerous terms of
IIC1
in a point-wise manner thereby allowing us to close the argument. This is an extremely important point to note about the special structure of the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations. Without this cancellation, we would not have an obstruction to a potential blow up of bad norms (that are not under control) in finite time. We first show this cancellation. Write down the expression for
\widehat{\nabla}^{2}_{3}\alpha^{F}
using the
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
transport equation for
\alpha^{F}
(once again we write down the most important term exactly and the remaining terms are written in an schematic way)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}^{2}_{3}\alpha^{F}\sim\nabla_{3}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}(-\widehat{\text{{div}}}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F})

(6.89)

\displaystyle+~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}(-\widehat{\text{{curl}}}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}^{F})

\displaystyle+\nabla_{3}\eta\sigma^{F}+\eta\nabla_{3}\sigma^{F}+\nabla_{3}\eta\rho^{F}+\eta\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}+\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}+\underline{\omega}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}

\displaystyle-(\nabla_{3}\widehat{\chi})\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\widehat{\chi}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+(\bar{\beta}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\bar{\alpha}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F}))

\displaystyle(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+\bar{\chi}\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}+\bar{\chi}\eta(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}).

\displaystyle IIC1+IIC2=-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F})-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}^{2}_{3}\alpha^{F}

(6.90)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}(-\widehat{\text{div}}\bar{\alpha}^{F})+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}(-\widehat{\text{{curl}}}\bar{\alpha}^{F})-\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}(\widehat{\text{div}}\bar{\alpha}^{F})+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{F}\cdot~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\text{curl}}\bar{\alpha}^{F}

\displaystyle+\widehat{\chi}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}\cdot\alpha^{F}+III=\widehat{\chi}\alpha^{F}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}\cdot\alpha^{F}+III,

where
III
denotes the collection of terms
L^{2}(S)
norm of which can be easily controlled by the available estimates.
\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}
can be estimated by commuting
\nabla_{4}
transport equation of
\underline{\omega}
with
hnabla_{3}
and the direct use of transport inequalities (proposition
5.2
). Using the
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
evolution equation for
\rho^{F}
, we can easily estimate the terms
\eta\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}
and therefore both
\eta\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}
and
\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}
are under control. Similar to
\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}
, there are other entities that arise which can not be reduced by means of transport equations. This of course includes
\nabla_{3}\eta
. Therefore let us obtain estimates for
\nabla_{3}\eta
and the estimates for similar entities such as
\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}
will follow in the exact same way. First commute the transport equation for
\eta
with
\nabla_{3}
to yield (schematically)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta\sim\nabla_{3}\chi(\eta-\underline{\eta})+\chi(\nabla_{3}\eta-\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta})+\nabla_{3}\beta+(\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F})\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})

(6.91)

\displaystyle+\alpha^{F}\cdot(\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F})+\omega\nabla_{3}\eta+\underline{\omega}\nabla_{4}\eta+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\nabla\eta+\sigma\eta+\Phi^{F}\cdot\Phi^{F}\eta.

Once again, we note that
\nabla_{4}\eta
terms may be eliminated by means of the
\nabla_{4}
transport equation for
\eta
. Similarly we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{3}\beta\sim\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta+\nabla\rho+~^{*}\nabla\sigma+2\underline{\omega}\beta+\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\beta}+(\eta\rho+~^{*}\eta\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+\bar{\chi}\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}-\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})+\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F}(\sigma^{F}-\rho^{F})+2\underline{\eta}_{a}(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+\underline{\eta}(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})),

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}\sim\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}+~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}-~^{*}\eta\sigma^{F}+\eta\rho^{F}+\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F},

(6.92)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\sim\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}-~^{*}\underline{\eta}\sigma^{F}-\underline{\eta}\rho^{F}+\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha^{F},

(6.93)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}\sim\widehat{\text{div}}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F},

(6.94)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}\sim\widehat{\text{curl}}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}^{F},

(6.95)

Utilizing these evolution equations, the definitions of
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}(S)
, the
L^{\infty}
estimate of the connection coefficients, and lemma (2), we note the following

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{3}\beta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.96)

Going back to (
6.91
) and utilizing available transport equations, we observe

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}.

(6.97)

The transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) applied to (
6.91
) yields

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(6.98)

which through Grönwall’s inequality leads to

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))e^{C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\underline{u}}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.99)

This inequality of course yields the estimate

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.100)

This later estimate (
6.100
) will be used in proving the next lemma. In an exactly similar way, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

(6.101)

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

(6.102)

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\omega||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.103)

These estimates will be extremely useful in the future.
Now we may estimate
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}
through the codimension-1 trace inequality (
6.153
)

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}).

(6.104)

Now we estimate
L^{2}(S)
norm of the underlined entities in the expression of
\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\mu
as follows

\displaystyle||IIA||_{L^{2}(S)}=||\nabla\chi\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\nabla\chi||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})||\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)},

(6.105)

\displaystyle||IIB||_{L^{2}(S)}=||\beta\nabla_{3}\zeta||_{L^{2}(S)}=||\beta||_{L^{4}(S)}||-\nabla\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\chi}\cdot(\eta+\zeta)+\underline{\omega}(\zeta-\eta)

(6.106)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}~^{*}\sigma^{F}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

\displaystyle||IID||_{L^{2}(S)}=||\nabla_{3}\eta\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}||\alpha^{F}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}

(6.107)

\displaystyle(||\rho^{F}||_{L^{\infty}(S)}+||\sigma^{F}||_{L^{\infty}(S)})

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

\displaystyle||IIE||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))(||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)})

(6.108)

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

\displaystyle||IIF||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))(||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)})

(6.109)

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

\displaystyle||IIG,IIH||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

(6.110)

\displaystyle||IIK||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))||\nabla\eta||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

Putting together all the estimates and use of the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
)

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C\left(||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S_{u,0})}+\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

(6.111)

yields

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.112)

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}.

Now we go back to the definition of the Hodge system (
6.51
-
6.54
) to obtain

\displaystyle\text{div}(\nabla_{3}\eta)=-\nabla_{3}\mu-\nabla_{3}\rho+[\text{div},\nabla_{3}]\eta,~\text{curl}(\nabla_{3}\eta)=\nabla_{3}\widehat{\chi}\wedge\underline{\widehat{\chi}}+\widehat{\chi}\wedge\underline{\widehat{\chi}}+\nabla_{3}\sigma.

(6.113)

This Hodge system yields the following elliptic estimate since
K
is under control

\displaystyle||\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.114)

where we have used the
\nabla_{3}
transport equations for the entities present in the right-hand side of the Hodge system (
6.113
). Substitution of (
6.114
) into the estimate (
6.112
) yields

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(6.115)

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}})

and since
\Psi
is not
\bar{\alpha}
or
\alpha
, we can write

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(6.116)

and therefore an application of Grönwall’s inequality yields

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\mu||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))e^{C(\mathcal{O}_{0}\underline{u})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.117)

since
\underline{u}\leq J
. Therefore after integrating the elliptic estimate (
6.113
), we have

\displaystyle||\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.118)

Proceeding in an exactly similar way we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}||_{L^{2}(H)},~||\nabla\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})},||\nabla\nabla_{4}\omega||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.119)

The estimates for the rest of the connection coefficients can be estimated directly through their transport equations since the remaining connection coefficients satisfy both
\nabla_{4}
and
\nabla_{3}
transport equations.

\square

In the following lemma we prove the
L^{4}(S)
estimates of the difficult derivatives (i.e., can not be estimated directly from null transport equations) of the connection coefficients.

Lemma 6.6

Let
\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)<\infty
, then the following estimate holds

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),~||\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

(6.120)

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),~||\nabla_{4}\omega||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.121)

Proof.
In order to prove these estimates, we use the co-dimension 1 trace inequalities for any field
\varphi
(be it a section of the gauge bundle or tangent bundle or mixed)

\displaystyle||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\left(||\varphi||_{L^{4}(\mathcal{\bar{S}}^{{}^{\prime}})}+||\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\varphi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}(||\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\widehat{\nabla}\varphi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right),

\displaystyle||\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\left(||\varphi||_{L^{4}(\mathcal{\bar{S}}^{{}^{\prime}})}+||\varphi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\varphi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(H)}(||\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\widehat{\nabla}\varphi||_{L^{2}(H)})^{1/4}\right).

We prove one of the connection coefficients. The rest of the connection coefficients can be handled exactly similar way. Let’s consider
\varphi=\nabla_{3}\eta
and write

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\left(||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{4}(\mathcal{\bar{S}}^{{}^{\prime}})}+||\nabla_{3}\eta||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(H)}(||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\widehat{\nabla}\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)})^{1/4}\right).

(6.122)

Note that every term except
\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta
on the right-hand side is estimated. In order to estimate this term we can differentiate the
\nabla_{4}
transport equation for
\eta
with respect to
e_{3}
. Such an operation yields (schematically)

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta=-\nabla_{3}\chi(\eta-\underline{\eta})-\chi(\nabla_{3}\eta-\nabla_{3}\underline{\eta})-\nabla_{3}\beta-(\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F})(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})

(6.123)

\displaystyle-\alpha^{F}(\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}+\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F})+\omega\nabla_{3}\eta+\underline{\omega}\nabla_{4}\eta+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\nabla\eta+\sigma\eta+(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})\eta.

Using the estimates from the previous lemma (6.2-6.6) and the transport equations we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}.

(6.124)

Here
\Psi
does not contain
\bar{\alpha}
and the Yang-Mills curvature components are estimated by means of
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}(S)
since they enjoy one order higher regularity than the Weyl curvature. Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\leq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.125)

Now if we plug this estimate into (
6.122
), we obtain

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.126)

Proceeding exactly a similar way, we obtain the remaining estimates

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),||\nabla_{4}\underline{\eta}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),

(6.127)

\displaystyle||\nabla_{4}\omega||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)).

This concludes the proof of this lemma.

\square

Lemma 6.7

Let
\varphi
be any connection coefficients belonging to the set (
\mbox{tr}\chi,\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi},\widehat{\chi},\underline{\widehat{\chi}},\eta,\underline{\eta},\omega,\underline{\omega}
), then the following estimates hold

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S)),~||\nabla_{4}\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.128)

Proof.
The proof is a straightforward consequence of the previous lemma (7) and the null evolution equations. The connection coefficients that satisfy the
\nabla_{4}
and
\nabla_{3}
null transport equations, we can directly estimate their
||\nabla_{4}\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}
since the terms on the right-hand side of such equations satisfy
L^{4}(S)
estimate. The connection coefficients
(\eta,\underline{\omega},\underline{\eta},\omega)
) that do satisfy only one of the
\nabla_{4},\nabla_{3}
transport equations, the previous lemma yields the result.

\square

Lemma 6.8

\mathcal{W}(S)\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),~\mathcal{F}(S)\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})
and also
||\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),||\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.
Recall the definitions of
\mathcal{W}(S)
and
\mathcal{F}(S)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(S):=\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||(\alpha,\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)||_{L^{4}(S)},~\mathcal{F}(S):=\sum_{I=0}^{1}\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}(\alpha^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S)}.

(6.129)

First, we prove the estimate for
\mathcal{W}(S)
. Recall the codimension-1 trace inequalities (remember
\widehat{\nabla}
acts as a usual covariant derivative on fields that are not sections of gauge bundle)

\displaystyle||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+||\Psi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\Psi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(H)}(||\Psi||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\widehat{\nabla}\Psi||_{L^{2}(H)})^{1/4}\right),

(6.130)

\displaystyle||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\Psi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\Psi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}(||\Psi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\widehat{\nabla}\Psi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right),

(6.131)

where the constants may depend on the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. We shall observe that
||(\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)||_{L^{4}(S)}
and
||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S)}
can be completely determined by the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. However, this would not hold true for
\alpha
and
\alpha^{F}
. However, this would not matter since we may use the trace inequalities (
6.130
-
6.131
).
We start with the Weyl curvature components. A direct application of (
6.130
-
6.131
) applied to
\alpha
and
\bar{\alpha}
yields

\displaystyle||\alpha||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W}),~||\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W})

(6.132)

since
||\nabla_{4}\alpha||_{L^{2}(H)}
and
||\nabla_{3}\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
are dominated by
\mathcal{W}
. Now since
((\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma))
satisfy
\nabla_{3}
equations, we may write

\displaystyle||\beta||_{L^{2}}\leq C\left(||\beta||_{L^{2}(S^{{}^{\prime}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||\nabla_{3}\beta||_{L^{2}}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle\sim C\left(||\beta||_{L^{2}(S^{{}^{\prime}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta+D\rho+~^{*}D\sigma+2\underline{\omega}\beta+2\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\beta}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+3(\eta\rho+~^{*}\eta\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\nabla}(|\rho^{F}|^{2}+|\sigma^{F}|^{2})-\bar{\chi}(\alpha^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\omega(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})+2\underline{\eta}(|\rho^{F}|^{2}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F}+|\sigma^{F}|^{2})+\underline{\eta}(|\rho^{F}|^{2}+|\sigma^{F}|^{2})||_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\right).

Notice that all of these terms are estimated by
\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}(S)
with a factor of
\epsilon^{1/2}
or
\epsilon
in front. Therefore

\displaystyle||\beta||_{L^{2}}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0}).

(6.133)

after choosing a sufficiently small
\epsilon
.
Now we repeat exact similar procedure for the remaining
\bar{\beta},\rho,
and
\sigma
to yield

\displaystyle||\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0}).

(6.134)

Now we want to use the trace inequality

\displaystyle||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\Psi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\nabla_{3}\Psi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}(||\Psi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\nabla\Psi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right)

(6.135)

for
\Psi=(\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
. Observe the following

\displaystyle||\nabla\Psi||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{S}|\nabla\Psi|^{2}\mu_{\gamma}du\leq C\mathcal{W}^{2}

(6.136)

and obtain

\displaystyle||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C\left(||\Psi||_{L^{4}(S^{{}^{\prime}})}+||\Psi||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\nabla_{3}\Psi||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\mathcal{W}^{1/2}\right).

(6.137)

Now

\displaystyle||\nabla_{3}\Psi||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(6.138)

and

\displaystyle||\Psi||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{S}|\Psi|^{2}\mu_{\gamma}du^{{}^{\prime}}\leq\epsilon C(O_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S)).

(6.139)

due to the uniform
L^{2}(S)
estimates for
\Psi=(\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
.
Putting everything together

\displaystyle||(\beta,\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}C(O_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0})

(6.140)

due to the smallness of
\epsilon
.

Now we estimate
\mathcal{F}(S)
in terms of
\mathcal{F}
. A direct application of the trace inequalities (
6.130
-
6.131
) yields

\displaystyle||\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}),~||\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}).

(6.141)

Now recall the
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
equation satisfied by
\rho^{F}
and commute it with
\widehat{\nabla}
to obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}=-\widehat{\nabla}\hat{div}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\rho^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}+\nabla(\eta-\underline{\eta})\bar{\alpha}^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}

(6.142)

\displaystyle+(\bar{\beta}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})+\bar{\alpha}^{F})\rho^{F}+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}-\bar{\chi}\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}+\bar{\chi}\eta\rho^{F}.

Now an application of the transport inequality (proposition
5.2
) yields

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(||\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\int_{0}^{u}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}).

(6.143)

Now notice each term on the right-hand side of (
6.142
) can be estimated in
L^{2}
by definition of
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{F}
and utilizing the estimates for the connections (lemma )

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C||\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}(S),\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S))

(6.144)

Since
u\leq\epsilon
. We may therefore estimate
||\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}
by means of the initial data i.e.,

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0}).

(6.145)

We obtain a similar estimate for
\sigma^{F}
since it verifies a
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
equation

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0}).

(6.146)

Of course, using the integration inequality, we also have

\displaystyle||\rho^{F},\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0}).

(6.147)

Now using the trace inequality we obtain

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C\left(||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S_{0,\underline{u}})}+||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\right.

(6.148)

\displaystyle\left.(||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})})^{1/4}\right)

where each term on the right-hand side is estimated in terms of
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),\mathcal{W},
and
\mathcal{W}(S)
and in addition we gain a factor of
\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
from
||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}
due to the estimates (
6.145
-
6.146
) i.e.,

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{C}(O_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}(S),\mathcal{W}(S))

(6.149)

and therefore

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})

(6.150)

after choosing sufficiently small
\epsilon
. However, for
\alpha^{F}
we can not make use of the
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
equation since it is not controlled on
\underline{H}
. Instead, we can make use of the
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}
trace inequality (
6.130
)

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,0})}+||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||^{1/2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||^{1/4}_{L^{2}(H)}(||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)})^{1/4}\right)

where every term on the right-hand side is under control

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{0}).

(6.151)

An exact similar argument for
\bar{\alpha}^{F}
yields

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{0}).

(6.152)

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

\square

Notice that we also have

\displaystyle||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{0}),~||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}_{0})

(6.153)

through a direct use of the trace inequalities (
6.130
-
6.131
).

7
Energy estimates for the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvature components

In this section we estimate the energy associated with the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvature components. This would complete the proof of the main theorem. Once the connection coefficients are estimated, it is straightforward to estimate the curvatures only we need to keep track of bad components (
\bar{\alpha}
and
\bar{\alpha}^{F}
) since they controlled only on an incoming null hypersurface
\underline{H}
. However as we have mentioned previously, the connection coefficients that are associated with these bad terms in the curvature estimate are controlled solely by the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. Therefore, we can safely utilize the Grönwall’s inequality to close the energy argument. There are several ways to obtain the energy estimate for the curvatures. One of the ways is to utilize the stress-energy tensors and associated divergence identities. Even though we do not have a canonical stress-energy tensor for gravity due to the equivalence principle, we can still use the Bel-Robinson tensor. Yang-Mills field, on the other hand, is equipped with its own canonical stress-energy tensor. Despite the fact that the utilization of the stress-energy tensor provides us with a direct physical insight into energy propagation (and possible concentration), we will not use it in the current context. Instead, we take a direct approach (the approach that exists in the traditional PDE literature). First, we write down the integration identities that are useful in the current context.

For a gauge invariant object
f:M\to\mathbb{R}
, the following integration by parts identities hold

\displaystyle\int_{D_{u},\underline{u}}\nabla_{3}f=\int_{H_{0}}f-\int_{H_{0}(0,\underline{u})}f+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}f(2\underline{\omega}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}),

(7.1)

\displaystyle\int_{D_{u},\underline{u}}\nabla_{4}f=\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}(0,u)}f-\int_{\underline{H}_{0}(0,u)}f+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}f(2\omega-\mbox{tr}\chi).

(7.2)

See proposition (
3.1
) for a proof.
We start the estimates with components of the Weyl curvature. Notice that we need to identify a suitable collection of the curvature components whose energy estimates generate a cancellation of principal terms. This should of course be possible since we have already established the manifestly hyperbolic characteristics of the Yang-Mills sourced Bianchi equations.

7.1
Energy Estimates for Weyl curvature

Lemma 7.1

The null components of the Weyl curvature satisfy the following
L^{2}
energy estimates

\displaystyle\int_{H}|\alpha|^{2}+2\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.3)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.4)

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{u}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\sigma|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.5)

\displaystyle 2\int_{H_{u}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.6)

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}

Proof.
The proof relies on the direct use of the integration by parts inequalities (
7.1
-
7.2
). First consider
(\alpha,\beta)
. Direct use of (
7.1
-
7.2
) yields

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\alpha,\nabla_{3}\alpha\rangle+2\langle\beta,\nabla_{4}\beta\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{H_{u}}|\alpha|^{2}+2\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}-\int_{H_{0}(0,\underline{u})}|\alpha|^{2}-2\int_{\underline{H}_{0}(u,0)}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\alpha|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}2|\beta|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)

and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\alpha|^{2}+2\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}(0,\underline{u})}|\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}(u,0)}2|\beta|^{2}

(7.7)

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\alpha|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})-\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}2|\beta|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)}_{ER1}

\displaystyle-2\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\alpha,\nabla_{3}\alpha\rangle+\langle\beta,\nabla_{4}\beta\rangle\right)}_{ER2}

We have to estimate the error terms
ER_{1}
and
ER_{2}
. Since
\alpha
is innocuous in the current context (recall we are trying to prove a semi-global existence result where the size of the domain transverse to
H
is small),
ER_{1}
and
ER_{2}
can be easily estimated in terms of the initial data and smallness of
u
. Nevertheless, we write down the estimates explicitly. Let us recall the definition of integration over the spacetime slab
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}:=\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}\int_{S_{u,\underline{u}}}\Omega^{2}\mu_{\gamma}d\underline{u}du

(7.8)

and therefore in the error term
ER_{1}
, we can estimate the connection coefficients in
\sup_{u,\underline{u}}L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})
and estimate
\alpha,\beta
by their
L^{2}
norm on
H
. In doing so we gain a factor of
u
(
\leq\epsilon
). Since
||\Omega||_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{S)}}\lesssim 1
, we have

\displaystyle|ER_{1}|\leq\sup_{u,\underline{u}}(||\underline{\omega}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})})\int_{0}^{u}||\alpha||^{2}_{L^{2}(H)}du

(7.9)

\displaystyle+\sup_{u,\underline{u}}(||\omega||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}+||\mbox{tr}\chi||_{L^{\infty}(S_{u,\underline{u}})})\int_{0}^{u}||\beta||^{2}_{L^{2}(H)}du

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

Now we have to estimate the error term
ER_{2}
. We do so using the Yang-Mills sourced Bianchi equations

\displaystyle ER_{2}=-2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\alpha,\nabla_{3}\alpha\rangle+2\langle\beta,\nabla_{4}\beta\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=-2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\underbrace{\langle\alpha,\nabla\hat{\otimes}\beta}_{PI1}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha+4\underline{\omega}\alpha-3(\widehat{\chi}\rho+~^{*}\widehat{\chi}\sigma)+(\zeta+4\eta)\hat{\otimes}\beta\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{43})\gamma\rangle+2\underbrace{\langle\beta,div\alpha}_{PI2}-2\mbox{tr}\chi\beta-2\omega\beta^{W}+\eta\cdot\alpha-\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha+4\underline{\omega}\alpha-3(\widehat{\chi}\rho+~^{*}\widehat{\chi}\sigma)+(\zeta+4\eta)\hat{\otimes}\beta\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\langle\beta,-2\mbox{tr}\chi\beta-2\omega\beta+\eta\cdot\alpha-\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})\rangle\right)+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\alpha,(\eta+\underline{\eta})\beta\rangle}_{IBP1},

where we note that the principal terms
PI1
and
PI2
cancel each other producing the extra term (
IBP1
) due to the integration by parts procedure. In addition due to
\gamma-
traceless property of
\alpha
,
\langle\alpha,(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{43})\gamma\rangle=0
. In order to estimate
ER_{2}
, we need to use the expression for
D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b}
. Since there is no delicate cancellation here, we will write this term schematically

\displaystyle D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b}\sim\langle\alpha^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{b}\alpha^{F}\rangle-\chi_{bc}\mathfrak{T}_{c4}+\eta_{b}\mathfrak{T}_{44}-2\omega\mathfrak{T}_{4b}

(7.10)

\displaystyle-(\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})-\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot(\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}+\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F})).

Now note an extremely important fact: since we do not have a
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}
equation for
\alpha^{F}
, we can only estimate this term using the definition of
\mathcal{F}
(this is one of the reasons we need to include
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}
term in the definition of
\mathcal{F}
). Noting
\mathfrak{T}_{44}\sim|\alpha^{F}|^{2},\mathfrak{T}_{4b}\sim\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})
we obtain

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\beta,D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b}\rangle|\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\beta||_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}(D)}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.11)

Using the estimates of the connection coefficients, we may estimate the remaining terms of
ER_{2}
. Finally we obtain

\displaystyle|ER_{2}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.12)

The most important point here is that we gain a factor of
\epsilon
from the bulk integral (i.e., the integral over
D
). Therefore, even though some of the connection coefficients (such as
\eta
and
\underline{\omega}
) are dependent on
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{F}
, they always appear as multiplied by a factor of
\epsilon
and therefore under control. We have the first estimate

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}(0,\underline{u})}|\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}(u,0)}|\beta|^{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.13)

or

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.14)

We proceed in an exactly similar manner in order to estimate the remaining curvature components. Next we identify the triple
(\beta,\rho,\sigma)
and write down the integral equations

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\beta,\nabla_{3}\beta\rangle+\langle\sigma,\nabla_{4}\sigma\rangle+\langle\rho,\nabla_{4}\rho\rangle\right)=\int_{H_{u}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}

(7.15)

\displaystyle-\int_{H_{0}}|\beta|^{2}-\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\rho|^{2}-\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\sigma|^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi),

that is,

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\sigma|^{2}

(7.16)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\beta|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)}_{ER3}

\displaystyle-2\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\beta,\nabla_{3}\beta\rangle+\langle\sigma,\nabla_{4}\sigma\rangle+\langle\rho,\nabla_{4}\rho\rangle\right)}_{ER4}.

Once again, using the estimates of the connection coefficients from lemma (2), we estimate the error term
ER_{3}

\displaystyle|ER_{3}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.17)

In order to estimate
ER_{4}
, we utilize the null Bianchi equations (
3.36
-
3.45
). Explicit calculations yield

\displaystyle ER_{4}=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\beta,\nabla_{3}\beta\rangle+\langle\sigma,\nabla_{4}\sigma\rangle+\langle\rho,\nabla_{4}\rho\rangle\right)

(7.18)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\beta,-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta+\nabla\rho+~^{*}\nabla\sigma+2\underline{\omega}\beta+2\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\beta}+3(\eta\rho+~^{*}\eta\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{3b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\sigma,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma-div~^{*}\beta+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot~^{*}\alpha-\zeta\cdot~^{*}\beta-2\underline{\eta}\cdot~^{*}\beta-\frac{1}{4}(D_{\mu}R_{4\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{4\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\rho,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\rho+div\beta-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha+\zeta\cdot\beta+2\underline{\eta}\cdot\beta-\frac{1}{4}(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{34})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\beta,-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta+2\underline{\omega}\beta+2\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\beta}+3(\eta\rho+~^{*}\eta\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{3b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\sigma,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot~^{*}\alpha-\zeta\cdot~^{*}\beta-2\underline{\eta}\cdot~^{*}\beta-\frac{1}{4}(D_{\mu}R_{4\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{4\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\rho,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\rho-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha+\zeta\cdot\beta+2\underline{\eta}\cdot\beta-\frac{1}{4}(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{34})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}(\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\sigma,~^{*}\beta\rangle-\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\rho,\beta\rangle),

where the principal terms cancel point-wise through the integration by parts procedure. In order to estimate
ER_{4}
, we need the expressions for the source terms involving Yang-Mills curvature. Using the Einstein’s equations
R_{\mu\nu}=\mathfrak{T}_{\mu\nu}
, schematically

\displaystyle D_{b}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{3b}\sim(\langle\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F},\rho^{F}\rangle+\langle\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F},\sigma^{F}\rangle)-\bar{\chi}\alpha^{F}(\sigma^{F}+\rho^{F})-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle-\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}+\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F})

\displaystyle+2\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+2\underline{\eta}(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\bar{\alpha^{F}}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})+\underline{\eta}(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F}),

\displaystyle(D_{\mu}R_{4\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{4\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}\sim\widehat{\nabla}(\alpha^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}-\alpha^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})-\chi(\rho^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\bar{\alpha^{F}}+\sigma^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})

(7.19)

\displaystyle+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\alpha^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}),

\displaystyle D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{34}\sim\langle\alpha^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}\rangle+\underline{\omega}|\alpha^{F}|^{2}+\eta_{A}(\alpha^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F}+\alpha^{F}\cdot\rho^{F})+\langle\rho^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle\sigma^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}\rangle+\underline{\eta}(\rho^{F}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\rho^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})

where we may use the null evolution equations for the Yang-Mills curvature components

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}^{F}=-\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}\underline{\eta}\sigma^{F}-2\underline{\eta}\rho^{F}+2\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha^{F}

(7.20)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha^{F}=-\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}+~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}\eta\sigma^{F}+2\eta\rho^{F}+2\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F}

(7.21)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}=-\hat{div}\alpha^{F}-\mbox{tr}\chi\rho^{F}-(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\alpha^{F}

(7.22)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}=-\hat{curl}\alpha^{F}-\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\alpha^{F}

(7.23)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}=-\hat{div}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F}

(7.24)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}=-\hat{curl}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}^{F},

(7.25)

Note that even though
\bar{\alpha}^{F}
appears in the source terms, we can estimate its point-wise norm on
S
by means of
||\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}
through Sobolev embedding. We only write down the estimates involving the potentially problematic source terms since the remaining terms are harmless

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\Psi,\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}\rangle|\leq\int_{0}^{u}||\Psi||_{L^{2}(H)}||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}\sup_{\underline{u}}||\nabla\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.26)

where
\Psi:=(\beta,\rho,\sigma)
and
\Phi^{F}:=(\alpha^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})
. Therefore the error term
ER_{4}
is estimated as follows

\displaystyle|ER_{4}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.27)

Putting everything together, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}}|\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\sigma|^{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.28)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

Now we collect the triple
(\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
and write down the associated integral equation

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\sigma|^{2}=\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\sigma|^{2}

(7.29)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\rho|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\sigma|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})}_{ER5}

\displaystyle-2\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\bar{\beta},\nabla_{4}\bar{\beta}\rangle+\langle\sigma,\nabla_{3}\sigma\rangle+\langle\rho,\nabla_{3}\rho\rangle\right)}_{ER6}.

Using the estimates for the connections (lemma),
ER_{5}
is under control

\displaystyle|ER_{5}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.30)

In order to estimate
ER_{6}
, we utilize the null evolution equations

\displaystyle ER_{6}=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\bar{\beta},\nabla_{4}\bar{\beta}\rangle+\langle\sigma,\nabla_{3}\sigma\rangle+\langle\rho,\nabla_{3}\rho\rangle\right)

(7.31)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\bar{\beta},-\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\beta}-\nabla\rho+~^{*}\nabla\sigma+2\omega\bar{\beta}+2\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\beta-3(\underline{\eta}\rho-~^{*}\underline{\eta}\sigma)-\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{43}-D_{3}R_{4b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{{D_{u},\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\sigma,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma-div~^{*}\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\chi}\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}-\zeta\cdot~^{*}\bar{\beta}-2\eta\cdot~^{*}\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{4}(D_{\mu}R_{3\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{3\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\rho,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho-div\bar{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\alpha}+\zeta\cdot\bar{\beta}-2\eta\cdot\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{4}(D_{3}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{33})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\bar{\beta},-\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\beta}+2\omega\bar{\beta}+2\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\beta-3(\underline{\eta}\rho-~^{*}\underline{\eta}\sigma)-\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{43}-D_{3}R_{4b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{{D_{u},\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\sigma,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\chi}\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}-\zeta\cdot~^{*}\bar{\beta}-2\eta\cdot~^{*}\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{4}(D_{\mu}R_{3\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{3\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\rho,-\frac{3}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\alpha}+\zeta\cdot\bar{\beta}-2\eta\cdot\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{4}(D_{3}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{33})\rangle\right)+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\rho,\bar{\beta}\rangle

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\sigma,~^{*}\bar{\beta}\rangle.

Once again, we need the source terms explicitly in terms of the Yang-Mills curvature components

\displaystyle D_{b}R_{43}-D_{3}R_{4b}\sim\langle\rho^{F},\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}\rangle+\langle\sigma^{F},\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}\rangle-\bar{\chi}\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+\chi\bar{\alpha}^{F}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}),

(7.32)

\displaystyle(D_{\mu}R_{3\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{3\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}\sim\widehat{\nabla}(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rho^{F}-\bar{\alpha}^{F}\sigma^{F})-\bar{\chi}(\rho^{F}\rho^{F}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\alpha^{F}+\sigma^{F}\sigma^{F})

(7.33)

\displaystyle+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\bar{\alpha}^{F}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F}),

\displaystyle D_{3}R_{43}-D_{4}R_{33}\sim 2\langle\rho^{F},-\hat{div}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle+2\langle\sigma_{F},-\hat{curl}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma^{F}

(7.34)

\displaystyle+(\eta-\underline{\eta})~^{*}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle

\displaystyle-2\eta(\rho^{F}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\rho^{F}\sigma^{F})-\langle\bar{\alpha}^{F},-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}\underline{\eta}\cdot\sigma^{F}-2\underline{\eta}\cdot\rho^{F}

\displaystyle+2\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha^{F}\rangle+2\omega|\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}-4\underline{\eta}_{a}(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}-\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F}).

Using these explicit expressions, we will obtain estimates of the following types

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\Psi,\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\Phi^{F}\rangle|\leq|\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\Phi^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}du^{{}^{\prime}}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.35)

\displaystyle|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}\rangle|\leq|\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}du^{{}^{\prime}}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.36)

\displaystyle|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\bar{\alpha}\varphi\rangle|\leq|\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\Psi||_{L^{2}(S)}||\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{2}(S)}||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S)}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}du|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.37)

where
\Psi:=(\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma)
and
\Phi^{F}:=(\alpha^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})
and
\varphi
denotes any connection coefficients. Collecting all the terms, we obtain

\displaystyle|ER_{6}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.38)

and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\sigma|^{2}=\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\rho|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\sigma|^{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.39)

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

Now we move onto the last estimate. This one is the most delicate one and crucially depends on the special structure of the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations. Collecting the pair
(\bar{\alpha},\bar{\beta})
and applying the integration identities, we obtain

\displaystyle 2\int_{H_{u}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}=2\int_{H_{0}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}2|\bar{\beta}|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)}_{ER7}

(7.40)

\displaystyle-2\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(2\langle\bar{\beta},\nabla_{3}\bar{\beta}\rangle+\langle\bar{\alpha},\nabla_{4}\bar{\alpha}\rangle\right)}_{ER8}.

Now notice the term
\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)
in
ER_{7}
. Technically we can estimate
\bar{\alpha}
in the
L^{4}(S)
norm since in this current settings, we do have
||\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{4}(S)}
under control (lemma

6.8
). However, we want to avoid using such an estimate since this issue will reappear at the level of higher order regularity as well (and in that case we will not have
||\nabla\bar{\alpha}||_{L^{4}(S)}
under control). Therefore we want to emphasize the special structure of this term. The connection coefficients
\omega
and
tr\chi
that are multiplying
|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}
in the bulk integral of
ER_{7}
satisfy
\nabla_{3}
transport equation and as a consequence, we could estimate these solely in terms of the initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
. In other words, we have

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)|\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{u}\int_{S}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}\Omega\mu_{\gamma}du\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})||\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}

(7.41)

or

\displaystyle|ER_{7}|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}

(7.42)

and therefore we can safely use Grönwall’s inequality to estimate
2\int_{H_{u}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}
provided we can control the bulk term
ER_{8}
. We do so now utilizing the null evolution equations

\displaystyle ER_{8}=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(2\langle\bar{\beta},\nabla_{3}\bar{\beta}\rangle+\langle\bar{\alpha},\nabla_{4}\bar{\alpha}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(2\langle\bar{\beta},-2\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\bar{\beta}-div\bar{\alpha}-2\underline{\omega}\underline{\omega}+\underline{\eta}\cdot\bar{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{3b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\bar{\alpha},-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}-\nabla\hat{\otimes}\bar{\beta}+4\omega\bar{\alpha}-3(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\rho-~^{*}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\sigma)+(\zeta-4\underline{\eta})\hat{\otimes}\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{4}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{34})\gamma\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\sim 2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\bar{\beta},-2\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\bar{\beta}-2\underline{\omega}\underline{\omega}+\underline{\eta}\cdot\bar{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{3b})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\underbrace{\bar{\alpha},-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}+4\omega\bar{\alpha}}_{A}-3(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\rho-~^{*}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\sigma)+(\zeta-4\underline{\eta})\hat{\otimes}\bar{\beta}+\frac{1}{2}(D_{4}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{34})\gamma\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+2\int_{D}\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\bar{\beta},\bar{\alpha}\rangle)

where notice that the principal terms once again cancel each other in a point-wise manner after integration by parts procedure. An extremely important point to note here is that due to
\gamma-
trace-less property of
\bar{\alpha}
, the term
\langle\bar{\alpha},(D_{4}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{34})\gamma\rangle
vanishes. This character persists in the higher order as well. Once again, we need to explicitly evaluate the source term to estimate this bulk integral

\displaystyle D_{b}R_{33}-D_{3}R_{3b}\sim\langle\bar{\alpha}^{F},\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle-\bar{\chi}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})+\underline{\eta}|\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}-2\underline{\omega}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})

(7.43)

\displaystyle-\widehat{\nabla}_{3}(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F}).

We note that we encounter a term
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}
which does not arise from a null evolution equation and this is precisely why we needed to add this term in the definition of Yang-Mills curvature energy. Observe that in the most dangerous term
A
, the connection coefficients
\mbox{tr}\chi
and
\omega
satisfy
\nabla_{3}
evolution equations and therefore using lemma
6.1
, they are controlled by means of initial data
\mathcal{O}_{0}
only (even though we could have utilized the
L^{4}(S)
bound of
\bar{\alpha}
at this level, we do not do so since in higher order we would not have the same privilege). The estimates for the bulk integral involves

\displaystyle|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\varphi\bar{\alpha}\rangle|\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),~|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\varphi\Psi\rangle|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.44)

\displaystyle|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),~|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\Phi^{F}\rangle|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),~|\int_{D}\langle\Psi,\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rho^{F}\rangle|\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.45)

\displaystyle|\int_{D}\langle\bar{\alpha},-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}+4\omega\bar{\alpha}\rangle|\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}},

(7.46)

where
\Psi:=(\bar{\beta},\rho,\sigma),~\Phi^{F}:=(\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})
and we have utilized
L^{4}(S)
estimate of
\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}
and
\Phi^{F}
(lemma
6.8
) together with the definitions of
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{F}
. Putting everything together, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}}|\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\bar{\alpha}|^{2}+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})||\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

\square

Now we need to obtain estimates for the higher-order energy associated with the Weyl curvature. Once we have completed the estimates for the Weyl curvature, we will move on to estimating the Yang-Mills curvature.

Lemma 7.2

The horizontal derivatives of the null components of the Weyl curvature satisfy the following
L^{2}
energy estimates

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.47)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\nabla\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\nabla\sigma|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.48)

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{u}}|\nabla\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\rho|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\sigma|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

(7.49)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}2|\nabla\bar{\beta}|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\nabla\bar{\alpha}|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.50)

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\bar{\alpha}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}.

Proof.
The proof relies on the fact that the structure of the Yang-Mills sourced null Bianchi equation is preserved after commuting with horizontal derivatives. We proceed in an exactly similar manner. First we consider the pair
(\alpha,\beta)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}

(7.51)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)}_{ER9}-2\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\alpha,\nabla_{3}\nabla\alpha\rangle+2\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla_{4}\nabla\beta\rangle\right)}_{ER10}.

Similar to the previous lemma
7.1
,
ER9
is controlled by using the estimates of the connection coefficients from lemma
6.1

\displaystyle|ER9|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.52)

In order to estimate
ER10
, we use the commuted null evolution equations

\displaystyle ER10=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\alpha,\nabla_{3}\nabla\alpha\rangle+2\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla_{4}\nabla\beta\rangle\right)

(7.53)

\displaystyle=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha)+\nabla\hat{\otimes}\nabla\beta+4\nabla(\underline{\omega}\alpha)-3\nabla(\widehat{\chi}\rho+~^{*}\widehat{\chi}\sigma)+\nabla((\zeta+4\eta)\hat{\otimes}\beta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\nabla(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{43})\gamma+[\nabla_{3},\nabla]\alpha+[\nabla,\nabla]\hat{\otimes}\beta\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}2\langle\nabla\beta,-2\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\beta)+div\nabla\alpha-2\nabla(\omega\beta)+\nabla(\eta\cdot\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+[\nabla_{4},\nabla]\beta+[\nabla,div]\alpha\right)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha)+4\nabla(\underline{\omega}\alpha)-3\nabla(\widehat{\chi}\rho+~^{*}\widehat{\chi}\sigma)+\nabla((\zeta+4\eta)\hat{\otimes}\beta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+[\nabla_{3},\nabla]\alpha+[\nabla,\nabla]\hat{\otimes}\beta\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,-2\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\beta)-2\nabla(\omega\beta)+\nabla(\eta\cdot\alpha)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+[\nabla_{4},\nabla]\beta+[\nabla,div]\alpha\right)+\int_{D}\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta}\nabla\beta,\nabla\alpha\rangle.

Where once again
\langle\nabla\alpha,\nabla(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{43})\gamma
vanishes due to the
\gamma-
trace-less property of
\alpha
and since
\gamma
is metric. This error term may be estimated by means of the following estimates for the individual terms

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\alpha,[\nabla_{3},\nabla]\alpha\rangle|\sim|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\alpha,\bar{\beta}\alpha+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\alpha+\bar{\alpha}^{F}(\rho^{F}+\sigma^{F})\alpha+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\nabla_{3}\alpha-\bar{\chi}\nabla\alpha+\bar{\chi}\eta\alpha\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon^{1/2}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\alpha,\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha)\rangle|=|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\alpha,\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})\alpha+tr{\bar{\chi}}\nabla\alpha\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{u}\sup_{\underline{u}}||\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\alpha||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u})}}||\alpha||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{u}(\sup_{\underline{u}}||\nabla\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\alpha||_{L^{4}(S)})||\nabla\alpha||^{2}_{L^{2}(H)}du^{{}^{\prime}}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}),

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\alpha,[\nabla,\nabla]\beta\rangle|\sim|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\alpha,K\beta\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{u}\sup_{\underline{u}}||K||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}||\beta||_{L^{4}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\alpha||_{L^{2}(S_{u,\underline{u}})}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}),

where we have estimated the Gauss curvature of
S
in
L^{4}(S)
through the null Hamiltonian constraint
K=\frac{1}{2}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\widehat{\chi}-\frac{1}{4}\mbox{tr}\chi\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}-\rho+|\rho^{F}|^{2}+|\sigma^{F}|^{2}

and the estimates from corollary (1) i.e.,
||K||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})
. Moving on with the other terms

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,[\nabla_{4},\nabla]\beta\rangle|\sim|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\beta\beta+\alpha^{F}\beta+\alpha^{F}(\rho^{F}-\sigma^{F})\beta+(\eta+\underline{\eta})\nabla_{4}\beta-\chi\nabla\beta+\chi\underline{\eta}\beta\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}(||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}(S)}||\beta||^{2}_{L^{4}(S)}+||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}(S)}||\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\beta||_{L^{4}(S)}+||\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}(S)}||\rho^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\beta||_{L^{4}(S)}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{u}(||\nabla\beta||^{2}_{L^{2}(H)}||\chi||_{L^{\infty}}+||\chi||_{L^{\infty}}||\underline{\eta}||_{L^{\infty}}||\beta||_{L^{2}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\eta+\underline{\eta}||_{L^{\infty}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\nabla_{4}\beta||_{L^{2}}

Now use the equation of motion for
\beta

\displaystyle\nabla_{4}\beta+2\mbox{tr}\chi\beta=div\alpha-2\omega\beta+\eta\cdot\alpha-\frac{1}{2}(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})

(7.54)

and

\displaystyle(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})\sim\langle\alpha^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{b}\alpha^{F}\rangle-\chi_{bc}\mathfrak{T}_{c4}+\eta_{b}\mathfrak{T}_{44}-2\omega\mathfrak{T}_{4b}-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}(\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot\rho^{F}+\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot\sigma^{F})

This is why we need
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}
in the derivative estimate.
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}
and
\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}
may be controlled by the respective null evolution equations

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}=-\hat{div}\alpha^{F}-\mbox{tr}\chi\rho^{F}-(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\alpha^{F},

(7.55)

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\sigma^{F}=-\hat{curl}\alpha^{F}-\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\alpha^{F}.

(7.56)

Now recall
||\varphi||_{L^{\infty}(S)}\leq C||\nabla\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}
and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}}\leq C\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}}||\nabla\rho^{F}||_{L^{4}}

(7.57)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\alpha^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}}\leq\int_{0}^{u}\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}}||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}}

(7.58)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

Therefore collecting all the terms together

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,[\nabla_{4},\nabla]\beta\rangle\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.59)

which indicates that this is a good term since we gain a factor of
\epsilon
. Among other non-trivial terms, we have the following

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla(D_{b}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{4b})\rangle

(7.60)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla(\langle\alpha^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{b}\alpha^{F}\rangle-\chi_{bc}\mathfrak{T}_{c4}+\eta_{b}\mathfrak{T}_{44}-2\omega\mathfrak{T}_{4b}-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}(\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot\rho^{F}-\alpha^{F}_{b}\cdot\sigma^{F}))\rangle.

Here we will encounter several terms of which the potentially problematic terms are estimated as follows

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\alpha^{F}\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\alpha^{F}\rangle|\leq\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\alpha^{F}||_{L^{\infty}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}}

(7.61)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}}\leq\epsilon C(O_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

and

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}\rangle|\leq\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\widehat{\nabla}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

Therefore these are essentially good estimates since we gain a factor of
\epsilon
. Using the
L^{4}(S)
estimates of the derivatives of the connection coefficients (corollary 2) and putting everything together, we have

\displaystyle|ER10|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.62)

and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

(7.63)

yielding

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\alpha|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}2|\nabla\beta|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{W}_{0})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.64)

Now consider the triple
(\beta,\sigma,\rho)
and write using (
7.1
-
7.2
)

\displaystyle\int_{H_{u}}|\nabla\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\nabla\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\nabla\sigma|^{2}=\int_{H_{0}}|\nabla\beta|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\nabla\rho|^{2}+\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\nabla\sigma|^{2}

(7.65)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\nabla\beta|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\nabla\rho|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\nabla\sigma|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)}_{ER11}

\displaystyle-2\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla_{3}\nabla\beta\rangle+\langle\nabla\sigma,\nabla_{4}\nabla\sigma\rangle+\langle\nabla\rho,\nabla_{4}\nabla\rho\rangle\right)}_{ER12}.

Once again, utilizing the estimates for the connection coefficients from lemma
6.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle|ER11||\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}).

(7.66)

Now we estimate
ER12
using the null evolution equations

\displaystyle-ER_{12}=2\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla_{3}\nabla\beta\rangle+\langle\nabla\sigma,\nabla_{4}\nabla\sigma\rangle+\langle\nabla\rho,\nabla_{4}\nabla\rho\rangle\right)

(7.67)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\beta,-\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta)+\nabla\nabla\rho+~^{*}\nabla\nabla\sigma+2\nabla(\underline{\omega}\beta)+2\nabla(\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\beta})+3\nabla(\eta\rho+~^{*}\eta\sigma)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\nabla(D_{b}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{3b})+[\nabla_{3},\nabla]\beta+[\nabla,\nabla]\rho+[^{*}\nabla,\nabla]\sigma\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\sigma,-\frac{3}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma)-div~^{*}\nabla\beta+\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot~^{*}\alpha)-\nabla(\zeta\cdot~^{*}\beta)-2\nabla(\underline{\eta}\cdot~^{*}\beta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{4}\nabla(D_{\mu}R_{4\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{4\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}+[\nabla_{4},\nabla]\sigma-[\nabla,div]~^{*}\beta\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\rho,-\frac{3}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\rho)+div\nabla\beta-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha)+\nabla(\zeta\cdot\beta)+2\nabla(\underline{\eta}\cdot\beta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{4}\nabla(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{34})+[\nabla_{4}\nabla]\rho+[\nabla,div]\beta\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\beta,-\nabla(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\beta)+2\nabla(\underline{\omega}\beta)+2\nabla(\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\beta})+3\nabla(\eta\rho+~^{*}\eta\sigma)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\nabla(D_{b}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{3b})+[\nabla_{3},\nabla]\beta+[\nabla,\nabla]\rho+[^{*}\nabla,\nabla]\sigma\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\sigma,-\frac{3}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\sigma)+\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot~^{*}\alpha)-\nabla(\zeta\cdot~^{*}\beta)-2\nabla(\underline{\eta}\cdot~^{*}\beta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{4}\nabla(D_{\mu}R_{4\nu}-D_{\nu}R_{4\mu})\epsilon^{\mu\nu}~_{34}+[\nabla_{4},\nabla]\sigma-[\nabla,div]~^{*}\beta\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\nabla\rho,-\frac{3}{2}\nabla(\mbox{tr}\chi\rho)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha)+\nabla(\zeta\cdot\beta)+2\nabla(\underline{\eta}\cdot\beta)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{1}{4}\nabla(D_{3}R_{44}-D_{4}R_{34})+[\nabla_{4}\nabla]\rho+[\nabla,div]\beta\rangle\right)+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\nabla\rho,\nabla\beta\rangle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle(\eta+\underline{\eta})\nabla\sigma,\nabla\beta).

Once again we control the potentially problematic terms that may arise from the coupled sector

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\nabla(D_{b}R_{34}-D_{4}R_{3b})\rangle

(7.68)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\widehat{\nabla}(\widehat{\nabla}_{b}(|\rho^{F}|^{2}+|\sigma^{F}|^{2})-\bar{\chi}_{ba}\mathfrak{T}_{a4}-\widehat{\nabla}_{4}(\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\rho^{F}+\bar{\alpha}^{F}\cdot\sigma^{F})+2\omega\mathfrak{T}_{3b}

\displaystyle+2\underline{\eta}_{a}\mathfrak{T}_{ab}+\underline{\eta}_{b}\mathfrak{T}_{34})\rangle

Collect the possible dangerous terms and estimate them by means of elementary inequalities and the available lemmas
6.1
-
6.8

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}\rangle|\leq\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{\infty}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\alpha^{F}||_{L^{2}}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\rho^{F}\widehat{\nabla}^{2}\rho^{F}\rangle|\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\nabla\beta,\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}\rangle|\leq\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}||\nabla\beta||_{L^{2}}||\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}}||\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\rho^{F}||_{L^{4}}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})
