(\bar{\alpha}^{F},\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}|^{2}=\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}(2\omega-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi)+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}|^{2}(2\underline{\omega}-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi})}_{ER25}

\displaystyle-2\underbrace{\int_{D_{u},\underline{u}}\left(\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle+\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}\rangle+\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}\rangle\right)}_{ER26}.

As usual notice that the connection coefficients multiplying
|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}
satisfy
\nabla_{3}
equations and there are completely determined by their initial data. Therefore we obtain

\displaystyle|ER25|\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})\sup_{u}(||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}+||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}).

(7.155)

For
ER26
we utilize the evolution equations

\displaystyle\int_{D_{u},\underline{u}}\left(\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle+\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}\rangle+\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\left(\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F},-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}(\mbox{tr}\chi\bar{\alpha}^{F})-2\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}(~^{*}\underline{\eta}\cdot\sigma^{F})-2\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}(\underline{\eta}\cdot\rho^{F})+2\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}(\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F})-\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}(\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha^{F})\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F},\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho^{F})+\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}((\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F})\rangle+\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F},-\widehat{\nabla}^{I}(\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma^{F})+\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}((\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}^{F})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F},[\widehat{\nabla}_{4},\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}]\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F},[\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}](\rho^{F},\sigma^{F})\rangle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F},[\widehat{\nabla}_{3},\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}]\rho^{F}\rangle

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F},[\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\nabla}^{I}]\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F},[\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}]\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle+\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\alpha^{F},((\eta+\underline{\eta})(\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}+\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F})\rangle.

Now we notice some of the key features of the commuted equations. Consider the
\widehat{\nabla}_{3}
commuted equation for
\bar{\alpha}^{F}

\displaystyle\widehat{\nabla}_{4}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}=-\frac{1}{2}(-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2div\eta+2|\eta|^{2}+2\rho-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\chi}})\bar{\alpha}^{F}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\nabla_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\eta}\cdot\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}\underline{\eta}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}-2\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\eta}\cdot\rho^{F}

\displaystyle-2\underline{\eta}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}+2\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\omega\bar{\alpha}^{F}+2\omega\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\alpha^{F}-\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\alpha^{F}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\mbox{tr}\chi+2\underline{\omega}\mbox{tr}\chi+2div\eta+2|\eta|^{2}+2\rho-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\chi}})\bar{\alpha}^{F}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\chi\nabla_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\rho^{F}-~^{*}\widehat{\nabla}\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}(-\bar{\chi}\cdot(\underline{\eta}-\eta)+\bar{\beta}^{W}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{T}(\cdot,e_{3}))\cdot\sigma^{F}

\displaystyle-2~^{*}\underline{\eta}\cdot(-\hat{curl}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\sigma^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot~^{*}\bar{\alpha}^{F})-2(-\bar{\chi}\cdot(\underline{\eta}-\eta)+\bar{\beta}^{W}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{T}(\cdot,e_{3}))\cdot\rho^{F}

\displaystyle-2\underline{\eta}\cdot(-\hat{div}\bar{\alpha}^{F}+\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\rho^{F}+(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F})

\displaystyle+2(2\omega\underline{\omega}+\frac{3}{4}|\eta-\underline{\eta}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}(\eta-\underline{\eta})\cdot(\eta+\underline{\eta})-\frac{1}{8}|\eta+\underline{\eta}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho^{W}+\frac{1}{4}\mathfrak{T}_{43})\bar{\alpha}^{F}

\displaystyle+2\omega\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}-(-\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}-2\underline{\omega}\underline{\widehat{\chi}}-\bar{\alpha}^{W})\cdot\alpha^{F}

\displaystyle-\underline{\widehat{\chi}}\cdot(-\frac{1}{2}\mbox{tr}\underline{\chi}\alpha^{F}-\widehat{\nabla}\rho^{F}+~^{*}\widehat{D}\sigma^{F}-2~^{*}\eta\sigma^{F}+2\eta\rho^{F}+2\underline{\omega}\alpha^{F}-\widehat{\chi}\cdot\bar{\alpha}^{F})

\displaystyle+[\widehat{\nabla}_{4},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}]\bar{\alpha}^{F}+[\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}]\rho^{F}+[\widehat{\nabla},\widehat{\nabla}_{3}]\sigma^{F}.

Similar to the previous case, after another application of
\nabla_{3}
produces
\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta
. But since this is at the level of top order derivative it contains
\widehat{\nabla}^{I}\bar{\alpha}^{F}
in addition to an algebraic term
\bar{\alpha}^{F}
. Now we may control
\bar{\alpha}^{F}
in
L^{4}(S)
and
\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta
in
L^{2}(H)
. This way we gain a factor of
\epsilon
. Similarly, we control
\nabla_{3}\eta
and
\nabla_{3}\underline{\omega}
in
L^{4}(S)
using lemma
6.7
. The most dangerous terms are estimated as follows

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\langle\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F},(\omega,\mbox{tr}\chi)\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\rangle|\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})},

(7.156)

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\nabla^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta\bar{\alpha}^{F}|\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{u}||\nabla^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\sup_{\underline{u}}||\nabla\nabla_{3}\eta||_{L^{2}(H)}\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}

(7.157)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

\displaystyle|\int_{\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}}\nabla^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}\nabla_{3}\varphi\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|

(7.158)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\sup_{u}||\nabla^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}\sup_{u,\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{4}(S)}||\nabla_{3}\varphi||_{L^{4}(S)}\leq\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F}),

where
\varphi
denotes the connection coefficients.
Collecting all the terms, we obtain

\displaystyle|ER26|\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}

(7.159)

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}

and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{u}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}|^{2}\leq\int_{\underline{H}_{0}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}|^{2}+\int_{H_{0}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}d\underline{u}^{{}^{\prime}}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\rho^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\sigma^{F}||_{L^{2}(H)}.

Smallness of
\epsilon
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})+\epsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})+\epsilon C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle+C(\mathcal{O}_{0})\int_{0}^{\underline{u}}||\widehat{\nabla}^{I}_{3}\bar{\alpha}^{F}||_{L^{2}(\underline{H})}d\underline{u}

Exact same procedure but commuting the e.o.m with
\widehat{\nabla}
in the second time yields the estimate for
\int_{\underline{H}_{\underline{u}}}|\widehat{\nabla}_{3}\widehat{\nabla}\bar{\alpha}^{F}|^{2}
.
This concludes the proof of the lemma and the energy estimates associated with the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvature.

\square

Corollary 7.1

\mathcal{W}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0}),~\mathcal{F}\leq C(\mathcal{O}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{0})
.

Proof.
A direct consequence of lemma
7.1
-
7.5
, Grönwall’s inequality and the fact that
u\in[0,\epsilon]
and
\underline{u}\in[0,J]
.

\square

This concludes the proof of the estimates for the Weyl and Yang-Mills curvature throughout
\mathcal{D}_{u,\underline{u}}
in terms of the initial data. Once these estimates are derived, we can choose the bootstrap constant
\Delta
(
5.1
) large enough (but finite) to close the argument since the final estimates do not depend on the bootstrap constant
\Delta
.

8
Concluding Remarks

Here we have obtained ‘semi-global’ estimates for the coupled Einstein-Yang-Mills equations in a gauge-invariant way (in the sense of Yang-Mills gauge theory). The null structure of the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations plays a crucial role in achieving this result. This lays the platform to study several problems associated with the coupled Einstein-Yang-Mills dynamics in the immediate future. As we have mentioned, the double null framework is appropriate for the radiation problem associated with gravity or/and Yang-Mills (they both have the same characteristics) equations. Since the Yang-Mills equations are themselves non-linear, they can counterbalance the gravity (e.g., in the case of regular soliton-like solutions). As a consequence, all three possibilities (black hole solution, regular solutions, and naked singular solutions) are open in the context of an evolution problem. Therefore it would be interesting to obtain sharp criteria demarcating all three regimes.

Another interesting perspective would be to study the Wang-Yau quasi-local energy
[
35
,
36
]
contained in the space-like domain bounded by the membrane
S_{u,\underline{u}}
. Ultimately, the formation of singularities is associated with the focusing of energy, and since the dynamics of the topological
2-
spheres
S_{u,\underline{u}}
is one of the central parts of the analysis of singularity formation, it is only natural to understand the evolution of the energy contained within it. Therefore, we need a definition of energy in a fully relativistic setting. Luckily as we have mentioned before,
[
35
,
36
]
constructed a notion of quasi-local energy associated with a topological
2-
sphere
S_{u,\underline{u}}
. It was proven in
[
37
]
that such quasi-local energy while evolving along the incoming null direction reproduces the Bel-Robinson energy and the matter stress-energy tensor (at different orders of course) at the limit of approaching vertex. Motivated by this result, it is only natural to study the evolution of this quasi-local energy in the outgoing null direction and observe the behavior in the focusing regime (note an expression of the quasi-local energy was obtained in
[
38
]
in the presence of a gauge field). We expect an alternate notion of trapped surface formation through the study of this quasi-local energy.

Lastly, we want to mention the fact that our estimates associated with the Yang-Mills curvature components are completely gauge-invariant. In particular, we obtain estimates for the fully gauge covariant angular derivatives. This essentially hints at an apparent similarity with the Maxwell and Yang-Mills theory despite the fact that the latter is a fully non-linear theory because the gauge covariant derivative
hides
the information of the connection and the non-linear coupling shows up only as the commutator of the fully gauge covariant derivatives. This does not cause a problem in the context of obtaining estimates since all the associated inequalities are formulated in terms of the gauge covariant derivatives (and the estimates required for a local existence theory for coupled Yang-Mills equations can be obtained in a gauge-invariant way; note that at the end one ought to choose a gauge and work with the equation for connection. However, our proffered choice of gauge is temporal gauge where the Yang-Mills equations take the form of a symmetric hyperbolic system and the spatial connection can be determined in terms of the gauge invariant norms of the Yang-Mills curvature). There is of course a physical motivation behind this. Since the double null framework in some sense encodes the information about the
physical
nature of the Yang-Mills fields, the choice of the gauge should not matter and one should expect to obtain gauge-invariant estimates as is done in the current context. In this framework of the gauge-invariant estimates, therefore, the exterior stability of the Minkowski space under coupled gravity-Yang-Mills perturbations is expected to hold. In other words, we conjecture “
Exterior stability of the Minkowski space holds under the coupled gravity-Yang-Mills perturbations
. Since we have developed the framework in this article, we want to pursue such stability conjecture in the near future.

9
